<lang>English</lang>
<sig>a 2</sig> <note>This is a "signature mark," used by early printers to keep the pages of a book in the correct order during binding.</note>

# INTRODUCTION

In my description or outline of the volume now presented, published in 1903 under the title <term>Key of Solomon</term> <note>original Hebrew: "Mafteah Shelomo"; original Latin: "Clavicula Salomonis"</note>, I remarked that the chief work which is supposed to have served as the oracle of all sorcerers throughout history was the famous *Key of Solomon* <note>original Latin: "Clavicula Salomonis." The "Key" refers to a book of secrets or instructions for magical operations.</note>.

King Solomon, who, according to tradition, was king of the whole world, and who, according to the statement in <term>Ecclesiastes</term> <note>A book in the Bible traditionally attributed to Solomon, reflecting on the meaning of life and the vanity of worldly pleasures.</note>, could render all the joys and delights of life subservient to his will, stood forth as the pattern and prototype for all who hankered after the things which they regarded as the pleasurable and desirable objects of existence.

The legend runs that it was by means of magical conjurations that King Solomon the Wise was enabled to procure for himself all the delights and pleasures which he sought.

Gedaliah Ibn Yahya <note>A 16th-century Italian Jewish chronicler and philosopher (1515–1587).</note>, referring in his book *The Chain of Tradition* <note>original Hebrew: "Shalsheleth-Hakabala"</note> to works of this description bearing on the magical art, cites one by name, *The Key of Solomon* <note>original Hebrew: "Mafteah Shelomo"</note>—the very equivalent of the Latin *Clavicula Salomonis*. To quote his words:

<note>original Hebrew: "ואומרים שזולת החבורים שעשה שלמה הכתובים בעשרים וארבע חבר גם כן ספרים על טבע האלנות והעשבים וחיות בהמות ועופות וחבר ספרים והשבועות נגד השטנים ונקראת מפתח שלמה :"</note>

> ‘It is said that, in addition to the works composed by Solomon as contained in Holy Writ <note>The "Twenty-Four" books of the Hebrew Bible.</note>, he wrote books on the nature of trees and plants, on wild beasts, animals, and fowl: he was further the author of writings and conjurations against Evil Agents in a work called *The Key of Solomon*.’

In the words of the great scholar of the Faust-legend, Karl Engel <note>A German scholar known for his 19th-century studies on the folklore of Faust and other legendary figures.</note>, Solomon plays the same **rôle** in ancient Jewish Magic as Faust does in the Christian; it is, therefore, not to be wondered at that modern books on magic are attributed to him.

In comparatively modern times what is supposed to be translations or adaptations of the original Hebrew *Key* appeared in various Romance languages <note>Languages derived from Latin, such as French, Italian, and Spanish.</note>, not to speak of German or English versions. With regard, however, to the Hebrew text itself, it seemed, as far as our knowledge went, to be altogether lost; it was, in fact, given up as lost. (Compare Mathers, page 5: *The Key of Solomon the King*, 1889.)

It chanced some years ago that I became possessed of a fine copy of the Hebrew manuscript of what appears to be this very *Key of Solomon*. I came across the volume among the books belonging to my father, the late Reverend Samuel Marcus Gollancz <note>1819–1900; a prominent rabbi and the father of the editor, Hermann Gollancz.</note>.

<summary>This page introduces the legendary origins of "The Key of Solomon," a magical text attributed to the biblical king, and notes the significance of discovering a Hebrew manuscript version after it was long considered lost to history.</summary>
<keywords>King Solomon, magic, Key of Solomon, Gedaliah Ibn Yahya, Faust-legend, Hermann Gollancz, Jewish folklore, grimoires</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction by Hermann Gollancz, where he details the physical state and scholarly value of the specific Hebrew manuscript he is publishing. He argues for its unique completeness compared to other versions of the *Key of Solomon*.</meta>

->BOOK OF THE KEY OF SOLOMON<-

I have since seen one or two collections of a similar character, but they are by no means in the same good condition or clear handwriting, nor at all equal in bulk to the manuscript before me.

Considering its remarkable value for the folk-lorist and antiquarian, I have now determined to produce the same in its entirety. In the following pages I have given an exact reproduction of the original manuscript in my possession, even as regards size; a facsimile by <term>collotype</term> <note>A photographic printing process used in the late 19th and early 20th centuries to produce high-quality, detailed reproductions of documents.</note> process—a method rendered the more necessary on account of the numerous diagrams and illustrations with which the manuscript is interspersed.

In these brief introductory remarks I do not propose to repeat the details which I published concerning the work in 1903; at the time, I intended this small publication to be a fairly full description, or better perhaps a skeleton outline, of this practically unique compilation. I shall but briefly summarize the conclusions then arrived at, and leave the reader to consult the original Hebrew for fuller information by the aid of the brochure which I issued ten years ago.

Furthermore, both in order to serve as examples of the contents of this work, and also as a guide in deciphering the Hebrew cursive script of an <term>Italo-Spanish</term> <note>Also known as Sephardic cursive, this is a specific style of Hebrew handwriting used by Jewish communities in the Mediterranean region.</note> character, in which this copy is written, I am giving in this Introduction a transcript in square Hebrew character of a few of the passages occurring herein, of some the text simply, of others text and translation, or translation alone, reserving the publication of a complete rendering of the original for another occasion. (Pagination is given throughout in the usual figures instead of the Hebrew ones found in the original.)

As far as the scope of this compilation is concerned, it is most varied in character. Its references extend to Philosophy, Medicine, and <term>Alchemy</term> <note>An ancient branch of natural philosophy that sought to transform matter—most famously to turn lead into gold—and discover the elixir of life.</note>; Astronomy and Mathematics; Theology and <term>Physiognomy</term> <note>The practice of assessing a person's character or personality based on their facial features or physical appearance.</note>; Logic, Music, and Politics.

The general impression conveyed by a perusal of the work itself, is that it is Jewish in tone. Some of the invocations read as beautiful and pure Hebrew prayers, and could only have been composed by a Hebrew, whilst the spirit underlying the invocations, as well as the formulae themselves, strike one at frequent intervals as non-Jewish. The Jewish character of the work as a whole is thus often affected by the foreign elements introduced. Some parts, in fact, read as translations from some Western source, or adaptations from Arabic sources. The Book, like most of these magical writings, is a true image of that syncretism <note>The merging or attempted merging of different religions, cultures, or schools of thought.</note> so characteristic of this class <insert>of literature.</insert>

The supposed Salomonic origin of the work, and Solomon’s <term>connexion</term> with the Magic Art, may be seen from passages such as the following:

> This is a composition from the house of Solomon, peace be upon him, written in two books (fol. 6 b) <note>original Hebrew: "Zeh hu hibur mi-be[it] Shelomo a"h ha-nikhtav bi-shnei sefarim"</note>
> This book belongs to Solomon, the chief of the wise men . . . . this book is the adjuration of Solomon . . . . this adjuration is from Solomon son of David, the Great King (ff. 43 a–43 b) <note>original Hebrew: "Ha-sefer ha-zeh hu le-Shelomo rosh ha-hakhamim . . . . zeh ha-sefer hashva'at Shelomo . . . . ha-shva'ah ha-zot mi-Shelomo ben David ha-Melekh ha-Gadol"</note>

The title-page of this copy (though mutilated as regards the chief word, for obvious reasons, as I have shown in my brochure, p. 17) points to King Solomon as author. It speaks of the book as that of ‘Solomon, son of David, King of Israel’,

Having established the manuscript's physical history, he now begins to transcribe and translate the opening of the text itself, which blends Jewish theology with Renaissance occultism.</meta>

->INTRODUCTION<-

who sat on the throne of the Lord, and reigned over those on high and those below, his wisdom being greater than that of all the children of the East’.

That our MS., over 200 years old, is but a copy of an older one is clear from internal evidence, as I have indicated in several passages of my former ‘*description*’ (pp. 16, 25, 36).

The opening passage of the MS. (*fol. 1 a*) is as follows :

<margin>With the help of God, may He be blessed <note>original: בעה״י, an abbreviation for Be'ezrat HaShem Yitbarach.</note></margin>

## From here begins the book

# Introduction to the Book

Behold, after we have desired the commands of Solomon—peace be upon him <note>original: הע״ה, abbreviation for Alav Ha-Shalom.</note>—and chosen to follow in his footsteps according to our ability, we first acknowledge the existence of one necessary Beginning. And know that in the Beginning was the Glory of the Lord and true thought, which prevents [falsehood] from the heart of the believer; and from a righteous deed comes strength and power. Solomon—peace be upon him—said: besides the one God, may He be blessed, and besides virtue and faith, from which one arrives at a unique spiritual work <note>original: סגולה, "Segulah," referring to a special property or talismanic power.</note>. There is one beginning and one perfection, yet it is divided into many things; and in this way the quantitative parts return to being a single whole, even though it is in an incomplete manner; thus from these also grow every virtue and power.

In the name of the true living God, who is <term>Alpha and Omega</term> <note>original: אלפ״א ואו״, the first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, symbolizing totality.</note>, the Beginning and the End: by the composition of the Seal of God, blessed be He, it is known in the first part from the vision of God; to the knowledge of the second part from the vision of the angels; from the subduing of spirits to the knowledge of the fourth part; and from the imprisonment of those in the lower depths <note>referring to the inhabitants of the underworld or demons.</note> to the knowledge of the fifth part. The angels are of three kinds: that is to say, celestial, aerial, and terrestrial. And the celestial ones are also of two kinds, some of whom serve the blessed God alone; and they are nine ranks: <term>Cherubim</term>, <term>Seraphim</term>, <term>Aralim</term>, etc., of whom mortals should not speak, neither by natural power nor by artificial power. And these shall not be called upon in any way, for they are always before the Lord and His glory in praise and law; and they shall never depart from His presence. Perhaps because the soul of humankind was created with them, and with them it can become enriched and prosper. Behold, the spirit, through the grace of the <term>Aleph</term> <note>referring to the first letter of the Hebrew alphabet, symbolizing the Oneness of God.</note> with its body, can look upon the wondrous Glory in truth, and praise them and recognize its Creator. And this recognition is not the recognition of God in His [full] glory and power, except in the way that humans and all the prophets knew Him. And it is proper that you should know that in this name <term>Aika</term> <note>original: אייקא</note> nothing evil is to be understood; for <term>Mano</term> <note>original: מאנו</note> in the Greek tongue signifies "Philosopher," and in the Christian tongue <note>original: בלשון נוצרי, likely referring to Latin or a Romance language.</note> "Wise Man," and in the Hebrew tongue "Scribe." And by these the wisdom of the wise is understood, for through them a person writes them, and by it are known things present, past, and future.

After this introductory passage come twenty-six ‘prayers’ (i. e. Invocations), some in pure Hebrew, others consisting simply of Cabbalistic names. I here reproduce the first seven with translation.

First Prayer: **AGLA** <note>original: אגלא</note>, Light of Truth and Life, the Merciful Judge, preserve me and help me by the power of mercy and long-suffering in this holy vision, and be Thou gracious unto me. Amen .'.

->First Prayer.<-

AGLA (the initial letters of the Hebrew words *Ata, Gibbor, Leolam, Adonai*, meaning, ‘Thou art mighty for ever, Adonai’), Light of Truth and Life, Merciful Judge, preserve me and help me by the power of Mercy and [Thy attribute] ‘Long-suffering’ in the course of this holy vision, and be Thou gracious unto me. Amen!

### Second Prayer.

**MONOUN** <note>original: מונן (Monoun); likely a corrupted Greek or mystical name, which Gollancz interprets as "O Witness!"</note>. O Lord, Holy One, Father of Power, God of the Universe, before whom are all the created ones and the uncreated, Source of all formations, whose eyes saw the non-existent world <note>original: העדר העולם (he-eder ha-olam), literally the "absence of the world" or the primordial state before creation.</note>, of whose beauteous grace heaven and earth are full, and whose ears listen to every creature. Before they existed, He saw them all; in His Book were all creatures inscribed, both gods <note>original: אלים (Elim). In this context, it likely refers to angelic beings or celestial powers rather than "gods" in a polytheistic sense.</note> and men. Endow me, Thy servant, this day with understanding, lowly pressed as I am both in body and spirit beneath Thy feet, for the sake of Thy Holy Spirit. Be gracious unto me, and raise me up, so that I may behold Thy Majesty. Grant unto my actions this day Thy blessing and the confirmation of Thy watchfulness; and may Thy holy revelation serve to enlighten me. Amen!

### Third Prayer.

**<term>TETRAGRAMMATON</term>** <note>The "Four-Letter Name" of God (YHWH), considered too sacred to pronounce in Jewish tradition.</note>. (The invocation contained on fol. 52 b* might be read in conjunction with this term.)

Look Thou down, O Lord my God, Merciful Father, for ever directing aright all existing things. Thou, O God, seest and lookest upon every act of man and his angels. Dwell, I pray Thee, this day upon my good and meritorious work. I beseech Thee imploringly that Thy abundant kindness be with me this day, to make manifest unto me this holy revelation; and that Thou mayest endow me to overflowing with strength and power for the sake of Thy great and Holy Name, Thou, my Lord God, who puttest Thy praise upon the lips of those that love Thee, Thy men of righteousness. Amen!

* In the name of **EL ELODI HOELOHIM** <note>God of the Gods</note> **ELAHI** <note>My God</note> **EHYH ASHER EHYH** <note>I Am That I Am</note> **JAH** **SHADDAI** <note>The Almighty</note> **ADONAI ZEBAOTH** <note>Lord of Hosts</note> **JUD HE VAV HE** <note>The individual letters of the Tetragrammaton spelled out: Yod, He, Vav, He.</note> **AGLA** <note>An acronym for "Atah Gibor L'olam Adonai" (Thou art mighty forever, my Lord).</note> **AGLAII AGLAT AGLAUT AGLATUN** <note>Variations or mystical permutations of the AGLA acronym.</note> **ALPHA AND OMEGA** <note>original: אלפא ואו (Alpha and Vav/Omega); the first and last letters of the alphabet.</note> **AGLA ON TETRAGRAMATON TON MATON RAMATON GRAMATON RAGRAMATON TERAGRAMATON TETRAGRAMATON** <note>A series of mystical diminutives or expansions of the name Tetragrammaton, common in medieval ritual magic.</note>, the Mighty One, God, **ELYON** <note>The Most High</note>, etc. : Finished.

<summary>This page presents the second and third preparatory prayers of the manuscript, emphasizing the practitioner's humility and the divine power of mystical names. It concludes with a powerful list of divine titles and Kabbalistic acronyms used to invoke spiritual authority.</summary>

INTRODUCTION

# INTRODUCTION

Fourth Prayer <note>original Hebrew: תפלה ד' (Tefillah 4)</note>. O my God, God the Creator, <term>Adonai</term> <note>Hebrew for "My Lord," a title used to avoid pronouncing the sacred four-letter name of God.</note>, Father of all hidden formations; O Merciful One, surrounded with majesty and light, who before the world existed governed all things: behold, I prostrate myself before Thy splendour and Thy mercies which cannot be told, that Thou mayest enlighten me by the power of Thy holy angels with Thy holy revelation and with a clear perception; and by the names of Thy holy angels may my mind and memory be enlightened by the holy effects of the vision and holy and clear appearance, Amen! O Lord of Truth <note>The manuscript contains a question mark here, suggesting the original transcriber or editor, Gollancz, was uncertain of the final Hebrew word.</note>.

->Fourth Prayer.<-

O my God, God the Creator, Adonai, Father of all hidden formations; O Merciful One, surrounded with majesty and light, who before the world existed governed all things: behold, I prostrate myself before Thy splendour and Thy mercies which cannot be told, that Thou mayest enlighten me by the power of Thy holy angels with Thy holy revelation and with a clear perception; and by the names of Thy holy angels may my mind and memory be enlightened by the holy effects of the vision and holy and clear appearance, Amen! O Lord of Truth (?).

Fifth Prayer <note>original Hebrew: תפלה ה' (Tefillah 5)</note>. **HEKLAISTAI** <note>A mystical "Name of Power," likely a corrupted Greek term or a specific angelic name used in the Solomonic tradition.</note>. Adonai, Holy One, Compassionate Father, who triest the hearts which are in heaven and on earth, the sea and the depths, and all that is in them; they unto whom Thou hast granted favour, Thou before whom no thought or deed can be hidden! By the glorified and holy names of thy angels, grant me my desire and the privilege of this revelation without evil imagining; try (favour) me in Thy lovingkindness. Amen!

->Fifth Prayer.<-

HEKLAISTAI. Adonai, Holy One, Compassionate Father, who triest the hearts which are in heaven and on earth, the sea and the depths, and all that is in them; they unto whom Thou hast granted favour, Thou before whom no thought or deed can be hidden! By the glorified and holy names of thy angels, grant me my desire and the privilege of this revelation without evil imagining; try (favour) me in Thy lovingkindness. Amen!

Sixth Prayer <note>original Hebrew: תפלה ו' (Tefillah 6)</note>. **AMPHIMAIKON** <note>A mystical invocation name.</note>. Merciful Creator, who formest all souls in abundance, and givest all the good things that are favourable <insert>pleasant ones</insert> <note>The Hebrew insertion "נַיים" (nayim) likely refers to "pleasant" or "sweet" things.</note>, ordering them and guiding them; hear now, and make me understand Thy blessings in a humble spirit and with crushed heart, to accomplish through the multitude of Thy kindnesses that which I desire. Amen!

->Sixth Prayer.<-

AMPHIMAIKON. Merciful Creator, who formest all souls in abundance, and givest all the good things that are favourable, <insert>pleasant ones</insert> ordering them and guiding them; hear now, and make me understand Thy blessings in a humble spirit and with crushed heart, to accomplish through the multitude of Thy kindnesses that which I desire. Amen!

Seventh Prayer <note>original Hebrew: תפלה ז' (Tefillah 7)</note>. **LMIHARAH** <note>A mystical name of God or an angel.</note>. All-powerful One, Father of Kindnesses, Guide of Creatures, Judge and Refuge, Lord of lords, who in Thy lovingkindness didst grant unto Thy holy one and those who love Thee wisdom and grace. Grant me now to see Thy splendor; Thou art the Judge of all and the Indicator. Enlighten my heart today in the chapters <note>The Hebrew text notes a possible correction: "in the lightnings" (be-varkai) instead of "in the chapters" (be-pirkei).</note> of Thy clarity and Thy purity, so that I may be able to see Thy face and the wonders of Thy glory, for in these I have desired. Amen!

->Seventh Prayer.<-

LMIHARAH. All-powerful One, Father of Kindnesses, Guide of Creatures, Judge and Refuge, Lord of lords, who in Thy lovingkindness didst grant unto...

->BOOK OF THE KEY OF SOLOMON<-

Thy holy and beloved one wisdom and grace; be gracious unto me that I may behold Thy splendour. Do Thou, judging and informing all things, enlighten my heart, I pray Thee, this day with the flashes <note>The original manuscript uses the Hebrew word "כברקי" (ke-varkei), meaning "like lightning flashes."</note> of Thy brilliancy and Thy purity, so that I may see Thy face and Thy tremendous glory, for in these things do I delight. Amen!

After the twenty-six 'prayers' we have, on folio 3 a, the following highly interesting prescriptions or formulae:

First **PAV VHV** <note>These are mystical permutations of letters, likely derived from the 72-fold name of God.</note> mentioned by Elisha <note>A biblical prophet and disciple of Elijah, often associated with miracles in mystical literature.</note>. **ABGITS MBKJIEṢ** <note>These letters constitute the first two lines of the "Ana B'Koach," a 42-letter name of God highly revered in Kabbalistic tradition.</note>:

This name, **APUTEL**, the priest <note>Referring to the High Priest (Kohen Gadol) of the ancient Temple in Jerusalem.</note> bore on his breast on entering the Holy of Holies <note>The innermost sanctum of the Temple, entered only once a year on Yom Kippur.</note> on the Fast <note>Yom Kippur, the Day of Atonement.</note> (or 'fasting'). It revives the dead, that is, when you make mention of it by word of mouth. When written on a brass or golden vessel, it rescues from all manner of weapon and loosens every form of evil, so much so that it will lay all evil, and have power over everything that is done in the world.

**BANUKIT़T**: Say this regularly after thy devotions, and thou shalt be afraid of no man.

**JH HV HV** <note>A variation of the Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of God.</note>: Elijah <note>The prophet Elijah, who according to tradition ascended to heaven alive and possesses secret wisdom.</note> delivered this to Elisha in writing; and when placed in water, and a man possessed of a devil <note>Likely referring to a "dybbuk" or a malevolent spirit causing illness or madness.</note> drank thereof, that man escaped; and if you give of that water to a woman in childbirth, she will speedily recover. If you engrave it on silver and say, 'Let the eyes of men be bound', you will become invisible.

**ABṢNIK**: Written and placed under the bed-pillow will bring whomsoever you wish in a dream, who will acquaint you with your heart's desire <note>A practice known as "She’elat Chalom" or "Dream Inquiry," seeking divine or angelic guidance while sleeping.</note>.

**NKB NAKAR**: This was the name of which Noah made mention against the waters <note>Referencing the Great Flood.</note>, and Abraham against the fire <note>Referencing the legend of Abraham being cast into a furnace by Nimrod and surviving.</note>.

**JCHSH SHASH**: Count three days, then say it twice every morning; and eleven times **CHANUNA**, and fourteen times in the evening outside the city, and thou wilt bring whomsoever thou desirest.

**ANAZ ANTSACH**: Write this on a small rod of hazel-wood and strike the sea or a river with it, and it will dry up.

**LALIN LALIN**: This will benefit those possessed by an evil spirit when they carry it upon them.

**YEHIYEH** <note>Hebrew: יהיה, "He will be," a divine name related to "I Am That I Am."</note>: Elijah gave this to Elisha; it is effective against all evil when carried by a person.

**TETRAAL**: This is good for a youth to see in a mirror <note>A form of "scrying" or divination using mirrors or polished surfaces.</note> by means of the word, and when he carries it upon him.

**BIT BIAT**: He shall be saved from all evil and from wicked men when they carry this.

End.

<page-num>ix</page-num>
<header>INTRODUCTION</header>

# INTRODUCTION

**ANAZA ANṢH**: Write this on the small twig of a nut-tree, and beat the sea or stream with it, and it will become dry.

**LALUN LLUN**: Is of avail for those possessed of an evil spirit, as soon as they are placed upon them.

**YHYH**: Elijah <note>A biblical prophet famous for his ascent to heaven and role in Jewish mysticism.</note> gave to Elisha, and is of avail against every ill, as soon as it is placed upon a man.

**ṬṬRAEL**: Is of value for the sight of a child, when pronounced and placed upon it.

**BJṬ BJAṬ**: When worn, delivers from all ill and from evil persons. The end.

At the foot of <term>folio 5 b</term> <note>A "folio" refers to a single leaf of the manuscript; "b" denotes the "verso" or back side of that leaf.</note> (3 lines from the bottom) we have the following:

> ‘Solomon remarks by Divine inspiration: “God alone is One, and there is one Faith only—the exalted one which the Creator desired to be revealed unto mankind and to be distributed among the multitude; inasmuch as the angel <term>Samaël</term> <note>In this context, Samaël is presented as a messenger of wisdom, though in other traditions he is often associated with the figure of the accuser or fallen angel.</note> said to Solomon, ‘This shalt thou give to the people of Israel’, that is to say, <term>Shmuiel</term>, the Great Prince, Chief of all the angels and all the Ten Classes <note>The "Ten Classes" refers to the traditional Jewish hierarchy of angelic beings, such as Seraphim, Cherubim, and Ophanim.</note>; and he gave it further unto others. And it was pleasing in the eyes of the Lord, and He commanded him to sanctify it, and adjured him in the following manner.”’ <note>original: "אמר שלמה בשפע אלהי, אחד הוא האל יחיד, אמונה אחת לבד המעלה אשר רצה הבורא לגלותה לבני אדם ולחלקה ברבים באמור המלאך סמ"אל לשלמה זה תתן לעם ישראל..."</note>

As I observed above, the chief word of the title-page, in fact of the title itself, has been mutilated, cut out; so that if it were not for the accident that the expression is repeated in the body of the work, we might have been at a loss to know the exact title of the work before us. This we have in <term>folio 6 b</term>, where the writer remarks: **"I will now begin, with the help of the Helper, the second book of the Key (MAPHTEAḤ)."** <note>original: "ואתחיל בעזרת העוזר הספר השני מהמפתחו" (Ve-atchil be-ezrat ha-Ozer ha-sefer ha-sheni me-ha-Maphteaḥ).</note> And it occurs twice in the Appendix (<term>folio 3 b</term>), once in the phrase: **"in the book of the Key which Solomon, peace be to him, composed"**. <note>original: "בספר המפתח שעשה שעל"ה" (be-sefer ha-Maphteaḥ she-asah She-A-L-H). The acronym stands for "Solomon, peace be upon him."</note>

This compilation then, according to its full title, would be known as **The Book of the Key of Solomon** <note>original: "ספר מפתח שלמה" (Sefer Maphteaḥ Shelomoh).</note>.

That the Magic Art was looked upon as a serious occupation, and was kept as an exclusive possession, may be gauged from the paragraph (<term>folio 6 b</term>) containing the following regulation or order:

> "I entreat and command everyone into whose hand this composition comes, that he shall not give it to any person unless he be modest, a keeper of secrets, and diligent in his actions in this work; and I adjure him by the Living God, the Creator of the universe, that just as he guards his own soul, he should guard this book so as not to reveal it to those who are unworthy; and if he does not listen to this, I will pour out my plea before Him who granted me this knowledge, that he may not succeed in all his actions and desires which he seeks to do. Amen, may it be His will." <note>original: "אחנן ומצוה אני לכל המגיע החיבור הזה לידו שלא יתנהו לשום אדם אם לא יהיה צנוע ובעל סוד וחרוץ במעשיו..."</note>

<meta>This page continues the introductory matter of the manuscript, providing a stern warning to future owners and transitioning from the general history to specific magical "operations" or "experiments," most notably the conjuration attributed to Simon Magus.</meta>

<page-num>x</page-num>
<header>## BOOK OF THE KEY OF SOLOMON</header>

> ‘I beg and command any one into whose hands this compilation may fall, that he will give it to no man unless he be of a retiring disposition, able to keep a secret, energetic in the performance of this kind of work ; and I adjure him by the Living God, the Creator of the Universe, that in the same manner as he would guard his own soul, he will guard this book, and not reveal it to such as are unfit. And should he not listen (to this admonition) I place my supplication before Him who has graciously imparted this knowledge to me, that He shall not suffer him to prosper in all the actions and desires which he seeks to bring about. Amen, May this be His will.’

The MAGIC CIRCLE <note>A ritual boundary drawn on the ground to protect the magician and contain summoned spirits.</note> which plays so important a part in these operations is described or referred to in various passages, such as <term>folios</term> 3 b and following, 9 a, 14 a–15 a, 23 b, 59 b, 65 b–66 a.

The <gloss>Experiment</gloss> <note>original: "ניסיון" (Nisyon). In this context, it refers to a specific magical procedure or ritual trial.</note> or ‘Operation’ (‘Practice’) is dealt with on folios 7a–8b, 9 a, 55 a, 56 b, 70 a–70 b ; and in the Appendix, folios 1 a–2 b, and 3 b and following. It may be of special interest to give in full, with a translation, the ‘Operation’ of **Simon Magus** <note>A biblical figure (Acts 8:9-24) who became a legendary sorcerer in medieval and Renaissance folklore.</note>, contained on folio 55 a:

An experiment of Simon: from him, it is required that it be performed on the day of <term>Jupiter</term> <note>Thursday</note> or <term>Mars</term> <note>Tuesday</note> in the evening. First, you must have a candle of virgin wax <note>Wax from a hive that has never been used for a previous purpose, symbolizing purity.</note> burning. Then, make a circle with a sword as seen below, and mark on the four sides the sign of Solomon and the seals of Jupiter and Venus as you will see further on. Write the names of the sides of the world—that is to say, East, West, North, and South. When this is done, stand in the middle of the circle and say this incantation three times, no more and no less:

"I adjure you, **Lucifer**, and all your companions, by the Living God, by the angels above and below, by [such and such] and in the name of [P-U-P] <note>likely a placeholder for specific secret names</note>, etc. Furthermore, I adjure you by **Babel** and **Zebuk** <note>original: "זבוך"; likely a variant of Beelzebub.</note> your lords. Furthermore, I adjure you by **Satan**, in whose hand are the keys of Gehenna <note>The Hebrew term for the underworld or Hell.</note>. Upon you I adjure you by **Lucifer** your king; I adjure you by the tower of the abyss; I adjure you by the Law of God <note>The Torah.</note> that you shall have no permission to remain in the air, nor under the air, nor on the earth, nor under the earth, nor in the water, nor under the water, nor in the heavens, nor under the heavens, nor in any place in the world. Instead, you must come immediately to this place—you, Lucifer, with your companions—or you must send three of your servants who shall tell me the truth of everything I shall ask of them, in the name of **Agla**, **Aglai**, **Aglata**, **Aglaot**, **Aglaton** <note>Kabbalistic acronyms for "Atah Gibor Le-olam Adonai" (Thou art mighty forever, O Lord).</note> and in the name of Alpha and Omega <note>The first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, written here in Hebrew phonetics.</note>, He-Vav-Yod-He <note>The Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of God.</note>, **Mahel**, **Elihi**, **Eloah**, **Sabaoth**, **Elyon**, etc."

And take care that you do not sleep, for immediately Lucifer will come with his companions, or he will send three messengers from among his companions or his servants, and they will say to you: "What do you want, what do you desire, and why have you brought us here?" Then you shall immediately ask of them three things which you desire, and they will tell them to you. After the answer, you shall give them license to depart in the known manner. This is proper to do on the day of Jupiter or Mars when the moon is increasing in light <note>Waxing moon.</note>. It should only be done in the months of February, May, August, or October, when the Sun is in equal number with the Moon if possible; and if not, let it be done when the Moon is at its full. This is a true experiment, tested by many. It is essential that you be very clean and pure in body and soul. Do not ask more than three questions, for if you ask more, they will not answer nor give anything. And know that whatever rank or kind of spirits they may be, they will not answer more than three questions. And I, the writer (so it was written), stood upon this experiment and it is true. You will find his circle at the top of the back of this page, and it is this:

<page-num>xi</page-num>
<header>INTRODUCTION</header>

## The 'Operation' of *SIMON MAGUS.*

The 'Operation' of SIMON MAGUS should be carried out on a Thursday or a Tuesday in the evening. In the first place, you should have a candle of <term>virgin wax</term> <note>Wax from a hive that has never been used to house a new swarm; traditionally required for purity in ritual magic.</note> burning; then make a circle with a sword (as is shown below), and mark the four sides with the mark of Solomon and the seals of Jupiter and Venus (as you will see further on); then write the names of the four sides of the world, <term>that is</term> East, West, North, and South. Having done this, stand in the middle of the circle, and say three times, neither more nor less, this form of conjuration:

> 'I adjure you, O Lucifer, and all thy associates, by the Living God, by the Angels above and below, by So and So, and in the name of A and B, &c.; I furthermore adjure you by <term>Belzebuk</term> <note>Beelzebub</note>, your Lord; I moreover adjure you by Satan, in whose hands are the Keys of <term>Gehinnom</term> <note>The Hebrew term for the underworld or Hell.</note>. I adjure you by Lucifer, your King; I adjure you by the mighty deep; I adjure you by the Law of the Lord, that you shall have no power to stand in the air, nor beneath the air; nor on the earth, or beneath the earth; nor in the water, or under the water; nor in the heavens, or beneath the heavens; nor in any place of the world; but that thou shalt come forthwith unto this place, thou, O Lucifer, with thy associates, or that thou shalt send three of thy servants, who shall tell me the truth concerning all that I shall inquire of them, in the name of <gloss>AGLA, AGLAII, AGLTA, AGLAUT, AGLTUN</gloss> <note>AGLA is a magical acronym for the Hebrew "Atah Gibor Le-olam Adonai" (Thou art mighty forever, O Lord).</note>, and in the name of ALPHA, <gloss>Omega</gloss> <note>original: "VΩ"</note>, HE, VJV, JUD, HE, MAHL, ALIHAI, <gloss>God, the Lord of Hosts, the Most High</gloss> <note>original: "ELOKIM, ZEBAOTH, ELYON"</note>, &c.'

Beware, too, that you fall not asleep; for immediately Lucifer with his companions will come, or he will send three of his messengers or associates who will say unto you, 'What dost thou want or desire and wherefore hast thou brought us?' Then at once ask of them three things that you wish, and they will tell you. After the reply bid them farewell in the manner known.

It is meet that you should perform this on Thursday or Tuesday while the moon is on the increase <note>Waxing moon</note>, and it should only be practised, if possible, in the months February, May, August, or October, when the sun and moon are equal in number [of hours (?)], otherwise at full moon. Now this is an unfailing experiment, tested by many; but it is requisite that you should be perfectly clean and pure in body and soul. Do not ask more than three questions; for if you ask more, they will not reply nor answer. Understand, too, that whatever grade or kind of spirits they may be, they never answer more than three questions.

I, the writer (so it was written), have tested this 'Experiment', and found it true.

The concluding statement in connexion with another such 'Operation' (folios 56 a-56 b), headed <gloss>An Operation tested and true</gloss> <note>original Hebrew: "נסיין מנוסה ואמיתי" (Nisyon Menuseh ve-Amiti)</note> contains these remarkable words: 'This Operation has been learnt from a certain demon who placed herself at the service of the writer (so it was written), and taught him this process, which is true.'

<header>BOOK OF THE KEY OF SOLOMON</header>

Another characteristic ‘*Operation*’ we have on ff. 70 a—70 b. It runs as follows :

> The Trial of **Barakon**. It can be performed in any place, with companions or without, but it is better without companions. First, you shall have a sword, sharp and very well-polished. With this sword, make a circle and prepare the necessary things so that you do not need to leave the circle after entering. When you enter, take the aforementioned sword and thrust it before you inside the circle. Afterwards, you shall have parchment made from an <term>unborn dog</term> <note>Likely parchment made from the skin of a canine fetus, a grisly requirement common in darker grimoires to ensure the "purity" or "newness" of the material.</note> and the blood of a <term>hoopoe</term> <note>original: "Hodo-hod"; a bird with a distinctive crown of feathers, frequently appearing in Solomonic legends as a servant of King Solomon.</note>. Write on this parchment with this blood these characters in two rows, one above the other—the second line below the first. Know that after you have spoken with the spirit the first time, you will no longer need the circle. When you wish to perform this trial, hold this parchment in your right hand and say this Psalm <note>The specific Psalm number is not provided in the text.</note> three times. Afterwards, say this conjuration: 
>
> "I adjure you, spirit called **Barakon**, by the faith you have with your company of demons, and by the power and truth of the Living God, the True and Merciful, and by that angel who shall speak with the voice of trumpets on the Day of Judgment, 'Arise, all men and women who are dead,' and shall say, 'Come, come, come to the upright judgment before God, the Creator of all.'
>
> "I further adjure you, Barakon, by the angels above and below, and by all the dominions and powers of the heavens, that you come quickly. I further adjure you, **Barakon**, by these holy angels and their ministers and their signs: In the name of **Raphael** and his minister, the Sun; **Gabriel** and his minister, the Moon; **Samael** and his minister, Mars; **Michael** and his minister, Mercury; **Zadkiel** and his minister, Jupiter; **Hanael** and his minister, Venus; **Kaphziel** <note>Also known as Cassiel.</note> and his minister, Saturn; that you appear quickly before me in human form without causing any harm to my body, my soul, or my limbs, by the power of the Living and True God, who shall compel you to come and answer me.
> 
> "I further adjure you by the twelve signs of the Zodiac <note>The Hebrew text uses the acronyms T-Sh-T, S-A-B, M-E-Q, G-D-D representing Aries through Pisces.</note> and by the power of the angel Gabriel who gave strength to Samson and who bestowed wisdom upon Solomon, peace be upon him. I further adjure you, **Barakon**, by the holy names of the Lord and by the name of His Son <note>original: "b’no"; likely referring to a Messianic concept or an angelic "Son of God" name common in kabbalistic magic.</note>, which name shall be called at midnight, and at its voice all the dead shall rise, both good and evil, in the twinkling of an eye. And by the name *Shakriz* <note>original: "Sh-Z-K-R"; a secret name of power.</note>, by which the Creator, the Lord God, shall cause all the stones of the world from all buildings to fall one against the other and be overturned and fight together, and then human creatures shall say to the mountains, 'Fall upon us!' and to the hills, 'Cover us!'
> 
> "By the name of the Ineffable Lord, by which God, after the Day of Judgment, shall slay Satan with my chosen ones; and all the wicked of heart shall be <term>judged</term> <note>The Hebrew "yivasha'u" literally means "be saved," but in the context of the "River of Fire," it likely refers to a process of purification or punishment.</note> in the <term>River of Fire</term> <note>original: "Nehar Dinur"; in Jewish mysticism, the stream of fire that flows before God's throne.</note>, and my chosen ones shall rejoice and exult in the glory of the Living God, and they shall be pleasant there, enjoying and resting in eternal rest.
> 
> "I further adjure you, **Barakon**, by the humble spirit with your companions, by the Lord who is blessed and glorified in His lofty chambers, the Awesome One in His world and the Heaven of Heavens, who is served by all creatures forever and for generations of generations, that you come quickly in a small human form.
> 
> "I further adjure you, **Barakon**, by the holy ministering angels and by all the names of the Lord, that you come quickly and that no delay or cause at all shall hinder you. I further adjure you, **Barakon**, by all the creatures of the Lord above and below (and the most excellent of creatures shall be specified) that you come immediately without delay in a small human form, without harm and without any trembling or fear at all, neither to my body, nor to my soul, nor to any of my limbs, etc. I further adjure you by the exceeding joy that the Lord gave to the angel Michael and the angel Gabriel in their holy rank."
> 
> And then immediately the spirit shall come, who is wise and obedient to your commands, and he shall tell you all your desires. He will come in human form every time you wish to speak with him, and you shall ask him whatever you choose, and he will answer the truth with humility and a willing soul.

### The ‘*Operation*’ BARAKON.

This may be carried out in any place, with associates or without, better without.
In the first place you must have a sword, very sharp and glittering, and make with
this sword a circle. Have all the requisites ready, so that you should not have to go
out of the circle, when you have once entered it. Having entered it, take the said
sword, and fix it in front of you within the circle. Afterwards you must have the

<page-num>xiii</page-num>

-># INTRODUCTION<-

...the hide of an <term>unborn dog</term> <note>Likely parchment made from the skin of a canine fetus. In medieval magic, materials from "unborn" animals were prized for their symbolic purity or untapped potential.</note> and the blood of a hawk <note>The previous page identified this bird as a "hoopoe" (Hodo-hod); the author here seems uncertain or is offering an alternative.</note>, and write on this hide with this blood these characters in two lines, one above the other. . . . . Understand that once you have conversed with the spirit, there is no further need for the circle; and if you wish to employ this test, you need only take the hide in your right hand and say this Psalm three times, after which repeat this conjuration:

> "I adjure you, O Spirit, called **BARAKON**, by the faith that is in you, together with your associate the demon, and by the power and truth of the Living God, true and merciful, and by that Angel who, on the Day of Judgment, shall with the sound of the trumpets say: 'Rise, all you men and women that are dead,' and he shall say, 'Come, Come, Come unto the righteous judgment before God, Creator of All.'
>
> "Furthermore, I adjure you, **BARAKON**, by these holy angels, their ministers, and their signs; by the name **Raphaël** and his minister, the Sun; **Gabriel**, and his minister, the Moon; **Samaël** and his minister, Mars; **Michaël** and his minister, Venus; **Kaphṣiel** and his minister, Saturn <note>These pairings represent the traditional Renaissance "Seven Governors," where archangels are assigned to the seven classical planets.</note>; (I adjure you) to appear before me quickly in human form without harming either my body or my soul and members, by the power of the Living God of Life and Truth, and He will subdue you so that you come to reply to me.
>
> "I next adjure you by the twelve Signs of the <term>Zodiac</term> <note>The twelve constellations of the celestial belt, used here to represent the entirety of the heavens.</note>, Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricornus, Aquarius, Pisces; and by you, O Gabriel, the angel who gave strength unto Samson, and who inspired with wisdom King Solomon, peace be to him! I further adjure you, **BARAKON**, by the Holy Names **ADONAI** <note>Hebrew: "My Lord."</note>, A. B., and by the name **CHUZU**, at the sound of which when proclaimed at midnight all the dead, both good and bad, shall rise in the twinkling of an eye; and by the name **SOTER** <note>Greek: "Savior."</note>, by means of which the Creator, **ADONAI ELOHIM**, shall cause all the stones of all the buildings of the world to tumble one against the other, so that they shall be overturned and engage in war together; then shall the human creatures 'say unto the mountains, Come, fall upon us, and to the hills, cover us' (cf. Hosea x. 8). . . .
>
> "And I adjure you by the <term>Ineffable Name</term> <note>The personal name of God (the Tetragrammaton), which was considered too holy to be spoken aloud by practitioners.</note>, with which **ELOHIM** after the Day of Judgment shall slay the Satan and his select ones, and all the wicked of heart shall be put into a stream of fire; whilst his own elect shall be glad and rejoice in the glory and pride of the Living God, and there they shall find their delight, and rest the everlasting rest.
>
> "Again, I adjure you, O **BARAKON**, by the spirit of Him on high <note>original: <term>העניו</term>, <term>העלין</term>; likely referring to 'The Humble One' or 'The Most High' (Elyon).</note>, together with your associates, also by the Eternal Being, blessed and glorified in the recesses of the high places, tremendous in His world and the heaven of heavens, served by all His creatures for ever and unto all generations, that you come speedily in the form of a little man. I further adjure you by the holy ministering angels and by all the Names of the Lord, that you come quickly, that no obstacle prevent you, nor any cause whatsoever. I further adjure you, **BARAKON**, by all the things created by the Lord on high or below..."

<summary>This page details the lengthy conjuration of the spirit Barakon, invoking planetary angels, the Zodiac, and various names of God to compel the spirit to appear in a non-threatening, human form. The ritual highlights the syncretism of Jewish, Greek, and Christian mystical traditions common in Solomonic grimoires.</summary>

It demonstrates the dual nature of Solomonic magic: one approach based on the majestic attributes of the Creator, and another based on the complex, hierarchical lineages of spirits and demons.</meta>

<header>BOOK OF THE KEY OF SOLOMON</header>

->(and he shall detail the foremost creatures)<-

"...that you come immediately and without delay in the form of a little man, without hurt, trembling, or fear whatsoever, either to my body or my soul or to any of my members. I again adjure thee by the extraordinary portion of joy which the Lord allotted to the Angel **Michael** and the Angel **Gabriel** <note>The archangels Michael and Gabriel are frequently invoked in these texts to command lesser spirits, acting as the 'authority' the practitioner borrows.</note> in the grade of holiness."

Thereupon immediately there will come the spirit that understands and listen to your commands, and tell you all you desire. He will come in human form at all times that you wish to speak to him; as for thee, ask whatever thou choosest, and he will answer truthfully, in humility, and with cheerful heart.

---

Of **Conjurations** we have the following typical form (from *folio 23 b-24 a*):

<note>original Hebrew: "עוד משביע אני עליכם בשם בורא את הכל..."</note>

"I furthermore conjure you in the name of the Creator of All, the Fashioner of All, and the Maker of All, the Possessor of All, the Beginning and End of All, possessing and none possessing Him, the Fashioner and not fashioning Him, the producer and none producing Him, Lord and none lord to Him, Ruler and none ruling Him, He the cause of existence, and none the cause of His Existence, the First and no first to Him, the Last and none after Him; the Creator, and all are His creatures; the Fashioner, and all are His formations; the Possessor, and all are His possessions; the Maker, and all things are made by Him; the Worker, and all are His works; Lord, and all are His servants; Ruler, and over all is His dominion; the Source of Existence, and all exist by Him; the Cause, and all is the effect of this Cause; all shall pass away, but He lives and remains to all everlasting, <term>Selah</term>! <note>Selah is a Hebrew word of uncertain meaning, often used in the Psalms to signal a liturgical pause or to emphasize 'forever.'</note> (I adjure you) that you stir not from this spot until you fulfil my desire and will, my command and request, which is So and So, etc."

Another class of conjuration, different in so far that it adjures by means of <term>Cabbalistic</term> <note>Referring to Kabbalah, a tradition of Jewish mysticism. In this context, it refers to the use of secret names and spiritual genealogies to exert power.</note> Names in place of being a direct appeal to the "Creator of All," will be found on *folio 45 a*:

<note>original Hebrew: "עוד נשביע אותך וננזור עליך פִילִיט (נ"א ביליד) בן אנט..."</note>

"We further adjure you and bind you, **Pilit** (otherwise known as **Bilet**) son of **Ant**, son of **Nashron**, son of **Shemalon**, son of **Minkuam**, son of **Yukar**, son of **Shimsha**, son of **Na’adam**, son of **Batel**, son of **Bashbash**, son of **Ishrud**, son of **Mara**, son of **Ashmedai** <note>Also known as Asmodeus, the 'King of Demons' in Jewish lore, famously bound by King Solomon to help build the Temple.</note>, son of **Shuniron** (also called **Shomron**), who were princes over all the angels and emperors; for he ruled in the days of **Solomon** and in the days of the *Prophetae* <note>original: "פרופיטאיי" - a Hebrew transliteration of the Latin word for Prophets.</note>—that is to say, the **Prophets**—and then by many deeds he ruled over you. I adjure you with strength and all those close to you. Furthermore, I adjure you, **Bilet**, that you come to my aid for the sake of the honor of your father **Ant**, and for the sake of the honor of your mother **Naamah** <note>In Jewish tradition, Naamah is often identified as a demoness of beauty or the sister of Tubal-cain; she is frequently cited as a mother of demons.</note>, with goodwill and not in anger, with joy and not in sorrow, etc., in truth and not in falsehood, to do that which I have desired, etc."

> 'We furthermore conjure thee and decree concerning thee **PILIT** (or **BILID**) son of **ANT**, son of **NASHARON**, son of **SHAMLON**, son of **MINIKUAM**, son of **JUKAR**, son of **SHAMSHA**, son of **NAADAME**, son of **VATALE**, son of **VASHVASH**, son of **ASHRAUD**, son of **MARA**, son of **ASHMEDAI**, son of **SHUNARON** (or **SHOMRON**), who were princes over all the angels and Caesars, for he ruled in the days of **Solomon** and in the days of the Prophets, and ruled over you by reason of great deeds—thee do we adjure by force and all who are near thee. We further adjure thee, O **BILT**, that thou come as a help for the sake of the glory of thy father **ANT**, and for that of thy mother **NAEMAH**, with favour and not in anger, with joy and not in sorrow, with truth and not with falsehood, to do that which I have desired, &c.'

<note>original Hebrew: "אני בילט הגדול..."</note>
I, **Bilt** the Great, the Eastern One, am ready to respond to you on every question that you ask, and to fulfill your will, your desire, and your longing entirely; ask of me and it shall be done immediately. I am **Ashmedai** <note>Also known as Asmodeus, often cited in Jewish lore as the king of demons who assisted Solomon in building the Temple.</note>, King of the South, the Great Philosopher. If you desire to know concerning things past, present, and future, behold I am ready to answer and to resolve your doubts for you.

A characteristic counterpart to such request will be found in the reply on *fol. 46 a*:

> 'I, **BILT** the Great, the Eastern One, am ready to respond to every request which thou makest, and to fulfil thy will, and wish, and longing entirely; ask of me, and it shall be done forthwith; I, **ASHMEDAI**, King of the South, the Great Philosopher. Wouldst thou know concerning the past, present, and future, behold I am ready to answer, and to solve all thy doubts.'

One of the most interesting, perhaps, of all the Conjurations, is the one occurring in the Appendix, *fol. 6 a-6 b*. It runs as follows:

->Especially effective for diseases of the eyes<-

In the name of the Lord God of Israel, who dwells between the Cherubim, may He appear. <gloss>(*sic*)</gloss> <note>The scribe likely intended to write "appeared."</note> The Holy One, Blessed be He, entered the Garden of Eden with the angels following after Him. They entered into the midst of the Garden of Eden with scowling faces, saying to one another: "Why is the Holy One, Blessed be He, scowling?" [Because] He is angry on account of the lowliness <note>meaning the suffering or affliction</note> of [Name] son of [Name] who has a wound in his eye or "bad waters" <note>A historical term for cataracts or glaucoma.</note>. The angels said to the Holy One, Blessed be He: "Just as it is in Your power to heal him, so may it be Your will to heal him" <gloss>(*MS.*)</gloss>. 

And I adjure this wound or these waters that are in the eye of [Name] son of [Name] in the name of the Lord of Hosts, the God of Israel, and in the name of **I Am That I Am**, and in the name of **Metatron** <note>The highest of angels in mystical tradition, often called the "Prince of the Presence."</note>, and in the name of the **Seventy-Two** <note>The "Shem HaMephorash" or 72-syllable name of God, believed to hold immense power over nature.</note> of the Holy One, Blessed be He, and in the name of **Adribon**, and in the name of **Shabchiya** <note>(another version: **Shabtai**, the spirit associated with the planet Saturn)</note>, and in the name of **Shemuiel** the Great Prince, and in the name of **Shamshiel** the Angel, and in the name of **Elijah the Prophet**, and in the name of **Michael** and **Gabriel** and **Raphael**, and in the name of **Hadraniel**, **Piskhon**, **Mitmon**, and **Sigron**—the holy angels—and in the name of all the angels on high, and in the name of the **Seraphim**, the holy **Chayot** <term>Living Creatures</term>, and the **Ofanim** <term>Wheels</term>, and in the name of the ten generations, and in the name of the ten Torah scrolls, and in the name of the seventy names, and in the name of the angels who serve before the presence of the majesty of the Holy One, Blessed be He, and in the name of bread, water, and wine, and by everything the Holy One, Blessed be He, created in His world: that if you be a film, or a growth, or water, or whiteness <note>possibly a leucoma or white spot on the cornea</note>, or disease, or a stone, or a thorn, or any darkness, or worms, you shall go away or die and depart from the eye and from these eyes; or whatever thing it is, it shall be erased and go out of these eyes—from the eyes to the flesh, and from the flesh to the bone, and from the bone to the skin, and from the skin to the hair, and from the hair to the earth—in the name of the Lord God of Hosts. 

This shall be performed three times a day, and each time he shall sprinkle [water] with his ring finger <gloss>(*sic*)</gloss> <note>The "kamitza" or ring finger was often used in medicinal rituals.</note> in the water and let one drop fall in the name <note>The translator suggests this means "into the eye."</note> and he shall do this for nine days or twenty-one days and he shall be healed with the help of God. Finished.

### *A Specific for Pain in the Eyes.*

> ‘In the name of the Lord, God of Israel, who sits upon the Cherubim. The Holy One, blessed be He, shone forth in the Garden of Eden, and the Angels after Him. When they had entered into the midst of the Garden of Eden, their faces were sad, and they said one to the other, “Why is the Holy One, blessed be He, troubled? He is angered on account of the low state of <note>The letters A and B here are placeholders for the name of the patient and their parent.</note> A, son of B, who has a sore or bad water <note>Likely a reference to cataracts or fluid buildup in the eye.</note> in his eye.” Thereupon the angels addressed the Holy One, saying: “As You have it in Your power to heal him, may it be Your will to restore him to health!”’ And I will conjure this sore or this water in the eye of A, son of B, by the name **Lord of Hosts** <note>original: ADONAI ZEBAOTH</note>, God of Israel, and by the name **I Am That I Am** <note>original: EHYH ASHER EHYH; the divine name revealed to Moses at the burning bush.</note>, and by the name **Metatron** <note>In mystical tradition, the highest of the angels and the celestial scribe.</note>, and by the seventy-two Names of the Holy One, and by the name Adribun, and by the name Shabhi (according to another reading, Shabti), and by the name Shmuiel, the Great Prince, and by the name of the angel Shamshiel, and by the name of Elias, Enoch, the prophet, and by the name Michael, Gabriel, and Raphael, and by the name of Hadarmiel, Pishon, Mitmon, Sigron, the Holy Angels, and in the name of all the angels of the Upper Region, and in the name of the holy **Burning Ones**, **Living Creatures**, and **Wheels** <note>original: SERAPHIM, HAYOTH and OPHANIM; these are the three highest ranks of angels in the celestial hierarchy.</note>, and in the name of the Ten Generations, and in the name of the Ten Scrolls of the Law, and in the name of the Ten <term>Sephiroth</term> <note>The ten divine attributes or emanations through which the infinite God interacts with the universe.</note>, and in the name of the angels that minister in the presence of the Exalted Holy One, blessed be He; and in the name of bread, water, and wine, and all that the Holy One, blessed be He, has created in His world; (I <term>adjure</term> <note>To command or charge someone under oath or under the threat of a penalty.</note> you) whatever you be, whether film or spot or water, or white, or malady, or grit, or thorn, any sort of darkness, or worm, that you go forth or die, and depart out of the eye, or from these eyes; or as regards any other thing that can be blotted out, that it go forth out of these eyes, from the eye to the flesh, and from the flesh to the bone, and from the bone to the skin, and from the skin to the hair, and from the hair to the earth, in the name of the **Lord** <note>original: ADONAI</note>, God of Hosts.’

Let him do this three times a day, and each time let him sprinkle with his third finger <note>Traditionally the ring finger, which was believed to have a direct vein to the heart and was often used for applying medicinal salves or holy water.</note> of the water, and drop one drop ‘in the Name’ <gloss>it seems to me—adds the copyist—the author meant ‘in the eye’</gloss>; let him do this nine days or twenty-one days, and with the help of God he will be healed. **The End.**

### Concerning the influence of the planetary system upon the life of man, we have, on folio 52 b, the following:

**Saturn** governs the bones of man's body, the <term>milt</term> <note>An old term for the spleen.</note>, the right ear, and the urinary parts.

**Jupiter** governs the liver, the left ear, and the ribs of man's body.

**Mars** governs the gall, the right nostril, the sinews, and the kidneys of man's body.

**The Sun** governs the heart and the right eye during the day, and the opposite <note>Meaning the left eye.</note> during the night, as well as the brain in the head, the arteries, and the right side of the body.

<header>INTRODUCTION</header>
<page-num>xvii</page-num>

<margin>The Jewish Theological Seminary of America New York 1914 Oxford</margin>

# INTRODUCTION

Venus governs the flesh and fat of the human body, as well as the private parts and the semen in man.
Mercury governs the tongue, the mouth, and the <term>sinews</term><note>Often referring to tendons, nerves, or the structural connections within the body.</note>.
The Moon governs the left eye during the day and the opposite eye during the night, the lungs, the throat, the upper bowel, the womb, and the entire left side of the body.

The details connected with the <term>fumigation</term><note>The practice of burning incense or herbs as part of a ritual or for purification.</note> relating to these planets, to which reference is made on folio 37 b, and more particularly the form of the Prayer, are specially interesting. We read:

> 'Red sandal-wood for the Sun, aloe for the Moon, pepper for Mars, <term>mastix</term><note>Mastic, a resin obtained from the mastic tree, often used in incense.</note> for Mercury, saffron for Jupiter, <term>costus</term><note>An aromatic root used in ancient perfumes and incense.</note> for Venus, brimstone <note>original: "sulfur"</note> for Saturn. Thereafter the priest shall bless them, and the priest shall say this Prayer: O God of Abraham, God of Isaac, and God of Jacob, put Thy blessing upon these perfumes, so as to enlarge the power of their fragrance and their efficacy, in order that no enemy shall be able to enter them, nor any other forms, in the Name of <term>Adonai</term><note>A Hebrew name for God, typically translated as "Lord."</note>, the Living God, and Ruler for generation upon generation. Amen!'

Among other interesting items we might note, that in the paragraph under the heading ‘Chapter on Theft’ (folio 70 b) there is the diagram of an eye on folio 71 a, surrounded on the upper side with the curious expressions <term>Got Magot</term>, which is the Old French spelling of <term>Gog Magog</term> <note>Biblical and legendary figures often associated with apocalyptic enemies or giants.</note>.

The last two prescriptions in the present collection are as follows:

->To see a lamp burning and moving in the midst of the water, take a wax candle and write these names upon it; then cast it into the water. And these are the names:<-
->**AIKA, AKVA, AKA, PTIYA.** <note>These are likely magical "voces magicae" or power names without a direct translation.</note><-

1. To see a light burning in the midst of the water, take a wax candle and write these names upon it; then cast it into the water. They are <term>AIKA</term>, <term>AKVA</term>, <term>AKA</term>, <term>PTIYA</term>.

To cause a king or a ruler, or whoever you wish to do your will, to dream: Before everything else, wash your face, your hands, and your feet, and recite the <term>Shema</term> <note>The central Jewish prayer "Hear, O Israel."</note>. Afterward, stand on your left leg and recite this conjuration three times: "I call upon you, the spirits appointed to cause men to dream, whose names are **Shanshimon, Shan’un, Shangadiel, Sangadiel, Tampaltay, Vishstrom, and the Prince Armaniel El.** And I also call to you, **Ba'al Zevuv** <note>Beelzebub, or "Lord of the Flies."</note>, **Ron, Ruhi, Kivish, Bintivesh**, and all your groups who are appointed over this. I call to all of you and I adjure you, I decree and establish over you by these seventy-two names <note>A reference to the 72-letter name of God derived from Exodus 14:19-21.</note> and by the name of two hundred and sixteen letters that emerge from the verses 'And he moved,' 'And he came,' and 'And he stretched out' <note>The three verses of Exodus 14:19-21, each containing 72 letters.</note> (and here one should mention all the names), that you shall go this night to <note>original: "פב"פ"; a placeholder meaning "So-and-so, son of So-and-so."</note> [the person's name], and stand over him, and terrify him, and make him dream, and show him my form. You shall say to him that he must be careful for his soul and do all my will—which is [state your request]—and if he does not do it, he shall die a strange death that very week. You shall make him swear in the dream that he will do so. And if he does not want to swear, you shall strike him and make him stand and terrify him until he swears to do so. And you shall make him dream all this night, dream after dream and a dream within a dream, until he desires completely and utterly to do my will. And let him come during the day to seek me (or let him send for me) specifically to do my will. And the spirit of the herald, the swift one before you called Bulim, and you..."

...you shall hasten to go after him to do this thing for him, just as you did for Laban the Aramean <note>A biblical figure, the father-in-law of Jacob, who was warned by God in a dream not to harm him.</note> and for Abimelech, King of the Philistines. I further decree upon you by the power of the Name that originates from "In the beginning" <note>original: "Bereshith"</note> until "and void" <note>original: "Vavohu"</note>, which is the Name of 72 <note>Referring to the Shem HaMephorash, a 72-syllable name of God derived from Exodus.</note> (and let him mention the Name), that you shall have no rest nor quiet, neither in heaven nor on earth, nor in the air, nor in the water, nor in fire, nor in any place in all the worlds that our Creator created, until you complete this matter according to my will and desire, by the Name that was written on the forehead of Aaron the Priest <note>A reference to the golden plate or "Tzitz" worn by the High Priest, inscribed with "Holy to the Lord."</note>, that you go immediately and at once. End.

2. To overpower a king or ruler, or whomsoever thou desirest, to do thy will.
Before all things wash thy face, hands, and feet; read the *Shema* <note>original: "Deut. vi. 4"</note> <note>The central declaration of Jewish faith: "Hear, O Israel: The Lord our God, the Lord is one."</note>; then stand on thy left foot, and repeat three times this Conjuration:

> ‘ I call upon you, ye demons, who are appointed to coerce humankind, whose names are SHANSHIMON, SHANAUN, SHANGDIEL, SANGDIEL, TAMPALTI, VVISHTROM, VHSHR, ARMNIEL, EL <note>original: "EL" meaning "God" in Hebrew, here used as a suffix for a spirit name.</note>; and I further call upon you, O BEEL ZEBUB, ZRON, ROHI, KIBAISH, BINTIVASH, and all your set appointed for this purpose. I call upon you and adjure you, I decree and ordain upon you by these Names of God, formed of the 72 (names), and by the Name of the 216 letters, being the sum-total of the three verses (of Exodus xiv. 19, 20, 21) beginning with the words *And he journeyed* <note>original: "ויסע"</note>, *And he came* <note>original: "ויבא"</note>, *And he stretched* <note>original: "ויט"</note>—let him make mention of all the names), that you go this very night to A, son of B, that you stand over him, and intimidate him, and overwhelm him, and show him my likeness, and tell him to beware of his life and do all my will, which is so and so; that should he not perform it, he will in that week die a sudden death. And thou shalt adjure him in a dream to do so. Should he be unwilling to swear, you smite him, and stand over him, and frighten him until he swears that he will do so. And you shall coerce him all that night by dream after dream and dream within dream, until he be entirely willing to do my bidding, and come during the day to seek me out (or send after me to call me) literally, so as to do my will. And the zealous and speedy runner shall run before you, namely BOLIM, and you shall hasten and go after him to do unto him in this matter as you did unto Laban, the Syrian, and unto Abimelech, King of the Philistines. I further decree upon you by the power of the Name compounded of (the words) "In the beginning" <note>original: "Bereshith", Gen. i. 1</note> till "and void" <note>original: "Vo-Vouhu", Gen. i. 2</note>, that is, the Name of the 72 (letters)—he should mention the Name—that ye shall have no rest, nor ease, neither in heaven, nor on earth, neither in the air, nor in the water, nor in any spot in all the worlds which our Creator hath formed, until ye complete this matter in accordance with my wish and desire, by the Name that was written on the forehead of Aaron, the priest—that ye go directly and immediately. Finis. ’

What offices the various Spirits are prepared to perform are as interesting as they are varied. For example, we read (folios 46 a—48 a) of such ready and valuable services as the following:

> ‘ Lo, we are prepared to revive the dead, &c. ’

> ‘ Lo, we are prepared to place ships and boats at your disposal, on the seas and on the streams as you desire, &c. ’

It also notes various supernatural services spirits can perform, such as reviving the dead and providing transport.</summary>

->INTRODUCTION<-

# INTRODUCTION

> ‘Lo, we are prepared to break the locks of wardrobes and safes containing hidden treasures, etc.’

> ‘Lo, we are prepared to obey your commands, and wherever you please there shall meet 10,000 or even a million men in arms, etc.’

There is a paragraph (folio 66 a <note>Folio refers to a leaf in a manuscript; "a" denotes the front side (recto).</note>) telling how ‘to shut up a spirit in a ring, so that you may converse with it by day and by night, and it will teach you if you wish all that can be done in the world’. There are directions (folio 67 a) for ‘rendering yourself invisible’; for ‘eluding prison and fetters’; telling how you can injure any enemy (folio 68 b); for one who has taken poison; for one petitioning a lord or ruler (folios 6 a–6 b, Appendix).

The divers other paraphernalia required in the performance of the magical acts, such as the knife, the sickle, water and hyssop <note>A plant used in biblical times for ritual cleansing and purification.</note>, light and fire, etc., are all minutely described; the conjurations for the days of the week, and the relation of the Signs of the Zodiac to the art of Magic, are set forth in detail.

The <term>Mystic Alphabet</term> <note>A series of symbols believed to hold spiritual power, often used in amulets.</note> or ‘Alphabet of the Angels’ is given on folio 1 a of the Appendix; and on folio 40 b there is, touching angels, an excerpt from the *Book of the Angel Rasiel* <note>original: *Sefer Raziel HaMalakh*; a medieval handbook of Jewish magic and mysticism.</note>—a work that has long been regarded as perhaps the standard compilation of <term>Jewish practical Magic</term> <note>The use of sacred names and rituals to influence the physical world, as opposed to purely theoretical or meditative study.</note>. We can adduce no better example in support of this statement than the final prayer contained in our *Key*, which, extending over nearly four pages (folios 71 a–72 b), apostrophizes God as the ‘King’, going through the entire Alphabet a greater or less number of times in order to describe His distinctive attributes.

A similar passage occurs in the *Book of Rasiel* (towards the end), but with this difference; whilst the *Book of Rasiel* gives about 140 forms referring to the Creator as the King of the Universe, the compilation before us has about 360 such forms. Our version is, therefore, more complete than that which occurs in *Rasiel*. Besides, the variations themselves are most interesting and instructive; and what strikes one at first sight is the use of expressions throughout this long passage in our *Key* identical with those occurring in the Jewish Prayer Book, the original source of which may be traced to the Hebrew Scriptures themselves.

H. G. <note>The initials of Hermann Gollancz, the scholar and rabbi who edited this version of the manuscript.</note>

<meta>Continuing from the previous page's list of parables, this page shifts to a theological discourse—written in Garshuni (Arabic in Syriac script)—explaining the relationship between Christ's sacrifice on the Cross, the debt of Adam's sin, and the practice of fasting.</meta>

He who was a debtor, what was he? Was he one who devours his people? By the cross, what would he be other than something else? And through death, resurrection? For our whole life was slumbering in death. His birth, which was also ordained, died in the... <unclear>something</unclear>. What is the benefit? He is grieved by His cross for the one struck down and crucified. For a very long time, he was in pain from what issued from him, just as he is waiting for life or resurrection. But from everyone who is a debtor, [it is required] that he pays, either by the cross or by both. If we go to his world or another, if we find the truth of the matter, it is that he sits under God—or sits also in whiteness—until all the wolves are given over. One is a debtor to his... <unclear>wolves</unclear> for corruption also from those who sleep in whiteness for the states also by it... <unclear>Da'un</unclear>. If he places it or gives thanks for all of them, then it is a greater time, which is from what is perceived or from a near truth that is useful, useful. He took from his fear <insert>the descent of our cross</insert> which is perceived, or rather, or in whiteness, or newness in whiteness. He places a burden from God, for his faith is for the one who remains, being in the image of Christ. If the beauty of his knowledge is by His cross, let him be careful and mindful there; understand truly.

---

Why is it that the water from the city of the truth is for the sake of sins? They used to say that from all sins comes death, from upon the King and His death.

---

And his reward was death, and his life was from God, for the sake of the cross of us all. And Christ became near to the city of possession.

---

And I, today, am from Him. Christ in the image of Adam, my possession. Christ became sin for the purpose of following him who was a debtor. It was necessary, out of hunger for Him, that life be in the blood; this is true.

---

A burden from God—that is, the water from the pain in them both—by His cross He became famous to the Christians. And if... <unclear>Za</unclear>... in my reading, he comes in his whiteness, which is in the image of Adam; in truth, Adam, by possession.

---

For this reason, it happens through fasting, which always strips away the spoils from everyone, in the image of Adam at his beginning. Just or more than him, because he who strips the spoils goes into death and into non-death. It was not in the blood, for death is not opened from a side of him like him. Sins from every sin are harvested from all sins. For this reason, I return the harvested one to the self, which is truly, truly because of fasting to fasting. In truth, he fasts because of the fast. There was no sin, there was no sin, there was no sin, except its sin passed into what the sin became because of it. It is difficult for it not to have a helper, except for the very truth: he who was in death shouted truly <gloss>Gold, O noble one!</gloss> <note>original: "Dahaba ya Karimo" - This appears to be a poetic or honorific interjection, possibly referring to Christ's purity or a specific liturgical refrain.</note> Then he mentioned that its meaning became night. He loves the truest sin, and that was to be fulfilled by Christ my God for the sake of the living, if they are successful with their wealth in the life of all. And it was other than that, or for it to be from God. It was the life of the harvested one; for this reason, from Christ I came. And it was for the living, the death of all, which is the true life. He is able to be for the sake of life. And behold, everything was from God because of a burden from Him in that. From those who became, it was the fast, and Christ was the hunger <gloss>regarding the Fast of Forty</gloss> <note>This refers to the Great Lent, the forty-day fast observed by Christians before Easter.</note> which is from the fasting. It was a sign for it in the truth. Always death from a debtor is a resurrection of the past, because the hunger of the past for a human, he is not able, because of him who was living from the truth. I am all life from before His creation, truly; that is, he has read that which is from all pain. He is able from all life from before God because of <gloss>the very truth</gloss>. From him who became successful, a burden in the nations [is] a sign that I am living, I am all this life, the truest sin which is in truth because of the fast of God before [us], just as by His cross. For this...

The teachers of those who are spoken of [say]: if you consider for them the place of <term>frailty</term> <note>original: "mḥilutha"; referring here to the inherent limitations or weakness of the physical human condition.</note>, because if a person is a building in that which he is, whether he is to be led or to be tossed about, let there be in them the <term>perfection</term> <note>original: "gmirutha"; a state of spiritual completeness or maturity.</note> of the body and of the soul, from that which is sustained by <term>necessity</term> <note>original: "anānqayā"; from the Greek "ananke," referring to the natural laws or compulsions of the physical world.</note>.

A suffering prayer <note>Likely referring to a prayer born of asceticism or deep longing.</note>, when it is not known to them, is indeed perfect, because one rejoices at all times when it is spread out so that they might benefit from these things. By the vision of these things which are found for those who are of His vision, the soul which is within is like that which is not [merely] in the vision of the soul.
