# In the Name of the Son, Christ

In the name of the Son, Christ, the Lord of all and the Life-giver of all. By His help, we begin to write this discourse concerning **worms** and **crickets**, so that they may depart from the land, from the plantations, from the gardens, and from the enclosures <note>original: "gzārathā"; often referring to a fenced field or a specific agricultural district.</note>.

And this is the form of that **heavenly sickle** <term>sickle</term> <note>original: "magaltho"; this can mean either a "sickle" or a "scroll." In this context, it refers to a physical diagram or amulet—the "Sickle of Saint Ephrem"—believed to "cut down" or drive away pests.</note> which descended from heaven to Saint Ephrem while he was in the region of Edessa. And the King of Heaven appeared and said to him: "Whoever wishes to cast out the worms and crickets from the land, the garden, and the enclosure, let him write this form."

<margin>By the power of the Son, it shall be released</margin>

And he shall place it in the center of the garden and the land before the time when the worms emerge. One should not place it before the time, but rather after they have appeared. And when he scatters these [written] scrolls upon the land, they shall depart from it.

->**And this is the form of the sickle**<-
->**which descended from heaven to Saint Ephrem**<-

And here you have the names of all the kinds of crickets and worms:
*   The first among them is called the Rain-Louse: 21
*   The Herald-Locust: 4
*   The Cricket: 10
*   The Watcher-Pest: 3
*   The Biting-Moth: 11
*   The Cankerworm: 22
*   The Eater: <note>original: "Akla"; likely a reference to the 'devourer' mentioned in biblical pestilence.</note>
*   The Migrator: 5
*   The Burner-Blight: 5
*   The Burrower: 20
*   And the rest of the crickets and worms that exist in the earth.

And our Lord said to Saint Ephrem: "Because of the sins of mankind, I brought these things upon the earth. But because our Lord, in His mercy for the world, had compassion on humanity, He gave you this sickle/scroll which descended from heaven, from the place which was the dwelling of the life of Adam, from the garden enclosure. And behold, from this day forward, my will shall not fail you, for this scroll which descended to you shall drive away all the destroyers of the earth. And when you cast it upon the land as I have told you, they shall depart from the land."

And at the time of prayer, you shall pray this prayer over it:

> O Lord God Almighty and Lord of all, You who by Your wisdom created everything, and by the breath of Your mouth established the heavens and the earth: O God, have mercy upon Your people and upon Your inheritance. Cast out these plagues which You have sent upon the earth because of our sins. O Lord, receive our prayer and deliver us from them.

And at the time when you cast this scroll/sickle upon the earth, you shall pray with this prayer:

> O Lord God, who created the world, and by Your wisdom placed the healing <note>original: "asya"; literally 'physician' or 'healing/medicine.'</note> of heaven and earth: deliver this land from worms, crickets, lice, and all the destroyers that exist in the land. Amen and Amen.

And when you cast it, you shall cast it at the four corners of the land, and all the destroyers and plagues shall depart from the land by the power of our Lord Jesus Christ. Amen.

# Again, we write concerning the life and conduct of the holy father Mar Isaac the Second, and what he said concerning his way of life.
## And how he came to the land of Egypt, to the holy fathers who were there.

<margin>That it was nothing else but as if from a plague that occurred in a time of ruin</margin>
Let us speak now of the Christian <term>conduct</term> <note>original: "dubbare"; a technical term in Syriac spirituality referring to the ascetic way of life and spiritual discipline.</note> of this saint; how he was called and moved toward the divine and monastic life. When he saw among them chosen men living in the fear of God, and the beauty of the virtuous life which is the Kingdom of Heaven, he yearned in his heart to draw near to the monastery and become a monk. When he came to it, they received him as one of their own. He was diligent in the work of fasting, prayer, and vigils, like a perfect and disciplined man. Although he was young in years, he was an elder in his mind and in his thought.

<margin>To him</margin>
Furthermore, he was originally from the East, from the city of Mosul, and lived in the days of the Persian King **Shapur** <note>likely Shapur II, who reigned 309–379 AD, a period known for both the flowering of Persian Christianity and intermittent persecutions.</note>. This father, **Mar Isaac**, loved the fathers there more than those who were younger than him. Because it was seen that he excelled in beautiful conduct, his name became famous in all regions. When the fathers in the desert of Egypt heard about him, they sent for him to come to them. When the holy father Mar Isaac heard this, he sent word back to them: "I am not able to come, because I have an elderly brother from whom I cannot be separated."

<margin>2</margin>
Whereupon the fathers from the desert said to him: "Then you should come along with your brother!" When Abba Isaac heard these words, he prepared himself to go to the desert of Egypt. When the time was fulfilled and he reached the desert of Egypt, he was received by the holy fathers with great joy. Abba Isaac stayed in the monastery of **Abba Macarius** <note>St. Macarius the Great, one of the most famous Desert Fathers of Egypt.</note>. He remained there with them in fasting, prayer, and great diligence; as we have said of him, even in the days of his youth, he was an elder in his mind. Because the fathers saw that he conducted himself beautifully in the way of monasticism, they gave him the leadership of the monastery, and he was ordained a priest and called the Father <note>original: "Abba"</note> of the monastery.

<margin>20</margin>
He also had another brother, as we mentioned. When this holy father Mar Isaac saw that his brother was conducting himself in virtuous things, he commanded that he also should become a monk. When his brother heard this word, he was called and became a monk. He lived with his brother in the monastery a holy and pure life. They became famous for divine work, prayer, and fasting day and night, and they were like lights within the monastery. Everyone who saw them praised God, who beheld them in such perfect conduct.

<margin>Of the administrator</margin>

## The Construction of the Machine

And if you make for it an open hatch higher than this, and when the sack is filled in its counterweight and its exit, it will pull. And strengthen that one with golden ropes <note>This likely refers to high-quality brass or gilded chains to prevent rust</note>, and if you merit it to a high degree and a spring, we are able.

And the load shall rise with great force that shall drive for you a copper wheel beneath which is water, and upon them a full force from below. And if you succeed in its immersion from its rotation in the water, then you will find someone wise in all the sciences.

And open its window to its rear, and this is its secret? To cast a weight straight, and there shall stand opposite it from behind a wheel, and this is its form: a complete wheel behind this very thing at its bottom. Or within that window near it on the left side on the outside, take an iron <term>spindle<gloss>the central axle or rod of a wheel</gloss></term> and at its end a small and strong wheel, and place its wheel upon the mouth of the box. And as strong as its placement in the immersion beneath which are these complete <term>lanterns<gloss>mechanical cogs or perhaps buckets on a water wheel</gloss></term> and in the lower [part]: after your pulling and to strengthen the second wheel. 

And if you make it by force with those wheels and with these weights which I have marked for you below, open, and this is its very form. The counterweight is sufficient for it on its face, and if you are not [careful], you will not succeed for the most part, even with your efforts; and not in your heart, for a "living spirit" and "another spirit" <note>This is a biblical allusion to Ezekiel 1:21, here used to describe the animating force or kinetic energy within the machine</note> that comes here with the wheels, and the spirit of the spindle comes in a restricted way, and its place is full of lanterns.

And these are the spindle tools and these are the weights. And you shall open this gate, and one must know and be cautious that it should not be [too] strong upon the <term>machine<gloss>the Hebrew word 'makhona' here refers to the mechanical structure</gloss></term> in its counterweight. And refine it, for it is in your hand that there should be on it a double [force] from the right. And it rises in immersion, and not force against the structure, but strengthen the weights. And there is a variation, and because of it, it will not go hard in the despisal of the whole. Another thing: that every man is worthy to engrave the three wheels so they understand how from everything, and when it is placed upon the structure according to its nature—whether complex or simple—its explanation and its righteousness [are revealed].

# The Word of Tradition: For the city of Aleppo, Ignatius of Colonia, the General <note>Syriac: *Stratelates*. A Greek military title for a high-ranking general, often used in hagiography for military saints.</note>

Truly, everyone who desires to drive away all the [pollution] of the body in the Name, let him fall [prostrate]. He is like Elijah <note>The prophet Elijah is frequently invoked in Syriac traditions for his power over the elements and spiritual purity.</note>, of all righteousness and life. Between us, the Word is spoken, gathered for the discipline of the three fruits, from that time until the end. To resemble what was said: "O man, from the second sanctuary, which is for you from God." Without unity, he seeks life. They said that in speech, a palace is not condemned under the change [of Aleppo].

I, who am called from the discipleship, fulfilling the command that he proclaimed, in his will I have surrendered. He says: "It is, it is [Aleppo]."

It is right, when such a thing is prepared, that the holy disciple who is called "the stable one" should be more perfect. He goes in victory to all praise, we then in peace. Just as we have seen, he does not pray from excess, but for us to be prepared, if he knows, if he lives. Also, when the two [leaders] of the city are in life, we offer prayer for everyone. I did not spare his body, and I wrote: "For us, the Word which is in the [purity], we also pray for all righteousness wherever it may be."

A man spoke and said: "I fell before the body like one dead," and he said to me, "Arise, fear not. I am a human being from among the sons of men, and the Merciful God saw that your body has suffered for a long time. He sent me to you to be a **healer** for all the blows and pains you have." And he delivered into my hands a bag full of living forces <term>living forces: *chaywata*. Can refer to vital spirits, small creatures, or the "living" essence of a disease.</term> and bindings <note>original: *tsamde*. Charms or amulets used to 'bind' a demon or a sickness.</note>.

And he said to this one who had separated from the hands of the physicians: "They are but students in the medicine of the body, but I am the physician of souls as well as the body. For I am the servant of the wise **Mar Behnam** <term>Mar Behnam: A 4th-century Persian prince and martyr-saint highly venerated in the Syriac tradition for his powers of healing and exorcism.</term>." And he also delivered into my hands the bag in which are all kinds of living forces. 

And he said to me: "Catch every distress that God pursues into the hidden places." And I saw that it casts out the spirit. And he said to them with a defiled <note>or "reproving"</note> voice: "You shall go out from his rays <note>likely referring to the patient's aura or the light of the soul</note>, and I, by the power of the servant, command every living force to go out from your **nostrils** <note>In ancient Syriac medicine, the nostrils were considered the primary entry and exit points for spirits and the 'breath of life.'</note>. And by the word of the Living God, the spirit shall fly from them."

The soul [shall go out] from their nostrils. Also, every demon that is bound among them shall fall like a dead man. And I command in this place every living force in which there is a soul [to go out] from their nostrils like rays. And the living forces shall fall from them like vinegar <note>Syriac: *chelta*. This may refer to dross or a sour discharge being purged from the body.</note>; it goes out like vinegar, it goes out like oil. And I said: "Ask the King from God."

### The Sweetness of the Vision

**You are strong, O prophet of God,** in this wondrous labor as God willed; if it increases for you in your authority, it comes forth from your mouth. And this prayer of God filled him with words.

<margin>You have confined</margin> as you have confined the fever <note>original: *chamreh*. Can also mean "heat" or "poison," often used to describe the burning sensation of illness.</note>, and God increases [it]. Our Lord said: "He guards you from all those who are evil in a dream of the night." And when the Elder <note>original: *Saba*. A title of respect for a monk or an elderly saint.</note> stood up, serving in the name of our Savior who casts light, the King is present. 

Everything that fell like a **reed of the river** <note>The reed (qanya) is a recurring symbol for fragility, but also for the measuring rod of God or a healing staff.</note>, his soul was cleansed from sin and forgiven him. The hand of **Hosea** <term>Hosea: Written as *Oshaya* in Syriac. This likely refers to a local saint or the biblical prophet invoked as a healer.</term> was stretched out with power, and it reached what had fallen under that authority from the light of God.

The prophet rejoiced in God the King, the Lord of heaven and earth. Hosea is stretched out beneath the whole wonder; he brought out for him a reed from the desert, stretched out like a tree. Every day, from joy to joy, where they are turned aside, there is no other person from within. There is no king who has not seen by the hands of the weary <note>or "the one who toils"</note> who was serving. 

After one hour <gloss>(eight hundred)</gloss> <note>This gloss likely refers to a numerical value associated with the prayer's efficacy or a specific year in the Seleucid era.</note>, God knew that in that hour [the hand] was stretched out. The standing which our Lord God gave to the Praiseworthy One; the reed of Hosea is guided. 

Before him sat the fullness of heaven, filled with standing in awe. And fear is spoken, praising the Living Lord who gives life to the dead and to the heart of the earth. 

Death willed to take Judas <note>Used here as a representative of doubt or the "adversary" who is defeated by the prayer.</note> who confessed in that division. He spoke it strongly as God saw, and the name of the evil ones, and the will of the earth. And God completed [it], and life is yours, for God does not neglect. 

Hosea saw that there is no vision [greater] than when he sees [Him]. And he blessed the strength of his wife <note>Possibly a reference to the biblical Hosea's narrative or a metaphor for the soul/church.</note> that she might descend in the way of God. 

All the virgins, when God is in prayer as she gives birth, say the name of our Lord, the Lord of heaven and earth. It was spoken by the kings of life; she took the vision of our Savior in this whole [matter]. How did you hear the word? 

**O Savior, our Lord, Hosea of God** at this time! And behold, Hosea stretched out his hands. Every person said: "If God hears the vision from all His mercy, we all rejoice in this from the Living Lord who is sufficient." 

From his mouth, the prayer to God was spoken from the whole heart, which fell into prayer as it was said. For in that hour, he arose from all his hope. The word of prayer was crowned in them, and he said to them at once: **"Our Lord is the Prophet of the Earth!"**

->**The Offering**<-

This chapter passes between the **Tree of Knowledge** and the **Tree of Life**, and speaks of the separation between **Judgment** <term>Judgment: *Din*, the divine attribute of rigor, law, and restriction.</term> and **Mercy** <term>Mercy: *Chesed*, the divine attribute of expansive love and kindness.</term>.

And this chapter alludes to those who perform the commandments <note>original: *mitzvot*. The religious deeds or obligations in Jewish law.</note>, for there is nothing between the Tree of Knowledge and the Tree of Life except a mere **hair’s breadth**; therefore, the door is open.

->**Lebanon**<-

->**The Intention**<-
<note>original: *Kavvanah*. This refers to the meditative focus or spiritual direction required during prayer or mystical practice.</note>

Know and understand that there is one spirit in the place of **M-H-Sh** <note>original: *MaHaSh*. This is one of the 72 Names of God, specifically the 21st triplet (Mem-He-Shin), traditionally associated with healing and the subduing of ego.</note>, and also in the hand of the Name <note>A common substitute for the Tetragrammaton (the four-letter name of God).</note>, may He be blessed, and within Him, blessed be He. This is the matter of all the places that the Lord chooses; He loved the good and hated the evil that was made in the **Foundation** <term>Foundation: *Yesod*, the ninth emanation (Sefirah) that acts as a funnel for divine energy into the world.</term> and the **World**. In His intellect, He is found in the name of **Judgment** and **Mercy**. He is strong and mighty so that the acts of Mercy should follow their proper order, according to the power and strengthening of His kindness. 

If Judgment is withheld from the chain of emanation, it returns to **Understanding** <term>Understanding: *Binah*, the third emanation, often viewed as the "Divine Mother" or the source from which the lower attributes emerge.</term>, and the aspect of this place would be restored. This was said to **Master Hananel** <note>Likely referring to Rabbi Hananel ben Chushiel, a prominent 11th-century scholar, though here invoked in a mystical context.</note> in the name of Heaven: that Judgment and Compassion do not remain in their [fixed] places, but Judgment will be strengthened for its own sake. They shall not return to mere chance; rather, the strengthening of Compassion is within Him and within His body. He said: "Let it be." 

And let Compassion be feared by Judgment, and He shall return Compassion to its secret order in every aspect. And if it finds favor in the eyes of Heaven, the word shall come to pass. In the extension of His kindness below, in the name of his trust, in his security, and in the expansion of His hand, in the name of his labor—therein the Lord is a mighty **Judge**.

# Question

**Question:** If a person prays this prayer with **diligence** and with **tears**: they shall triumph before Him all their days. And from the power of all sins, not even a bit of their foolishness shall harm them; they will be kept triumphant all their days through this prayer. For it is thus known in this manner: that if one prays according to the custom of this service, he shall truly see that through prayer—for the **Sons of Light** <note>Sons of Light: A term used in early mystical and Syriac traditions to describe those who are spiritually awakened or belonging to the divine realm.</note> are in this prayer—and when they pray it especially, they shall see a prayer such as this. For **our Lord** said that those who pray went out for my sake according to their victory; and to every form of prayer, if you are persistent, show it. With those who pray and stand in prayer, this is their prayer: as if there is something they pray in the prayer that is heard before the face of the one who spoke.

And because it is beautiful: we shall stand in this prayer every day. There are various kinds of prayer; for there are those who pray before their Lord without a command, nor is it excessive, for truly there are none who say "O" <note>original: "ya". A vocative particle used in direct address to God.</note> in their prayer who pray like the prayer spoken in this prayer. It is performed at the times of prayer, from **morning** to morning, and at the times of **evening** <note>original: "safra" (morning) and "ramsha" (evening). These refer to the standard liturgical hours of the Syriac tradition.</note>, two or three times like this prayer. Because we do not have to pray every day, O this prayer, so that our prayer may be accepted before the Lord: under the **Cherubim** <term>Cherubim: High-ranking angelic beings, often depicted in mystical texts as guardians of the divine throne whose wings carry the prayers of the faithful upward.</term>.

The truly worshiped, **King of the Syrians** <note>original: "Malka d-Suryaya." This likely refers to a specific holy figure or a title for Christ within the Syriac-speaking community, emphasizing the cultural lineage of the text.</note>, the living one who is in this prayer, he is the prayer of all their worship; and a great prayer they are. [O] sinful man, our Savior [is] in this prayer of the morning; because as one who is praised every day prays in this prayer, that in prayer, and in fasting, and in praises, they persuaded God. And He will receive your prayer as He wills, and save us all by His mercies, so that in this prayer there shall be joy and gladness for all the gatherings of the world.

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion of the Gospel of Matthew, but transitions abruptly into a Samaritan liturgical text. The following content reflects the Samaritan tradition's use of the Priestly Blessing and their unique synthesis of Hebrew and Arabic linguistic elements in prayer.</meta>

Freedom and freedom, and a resting place for the Unique One; for He is the Living Name <note>original: "Ashem". The Samaritans, like Jews, often use "The Name" to refer to God to avoid pronouncing the sacred Tetragrammaton.</note>, and according to him who is bound to Him. May I be with him entirely, unless he is created in the dwelling of the <term>Ishmaelites</term> <note>original: "Ishmaels". This likely refers to the Arab-Muslim world in which the Samaritans lived and wrote.</note>. May He lead me in goodness throughout the ages, for God has filled me with Your help and Your mercy. 

The house of the petitioner is in flight, and the desire has gone toward what I have provided. God is the only Living One; He does not change what is prepared. In His hand is the palm and the head, and he shall be delivered. May You be sweetened with me, for He is the foundation in His mercy. The Blessed One provides a path away from vanity. Go to Him, and between the Great Peace which is praised, may the memory of God be for him, for <term>Fatima</term> <note>The mention of Fatima here is unusual for a Hebrew text and reflects the deep cultural integration of Samaritan scribes within an Islamic environment.</note>, for the Guarded One, and for the <term>Sheikh</term> <note>A title of respect, here likely referring to a community leader or a specific religious figure.</note>. The peace of God be upon you all. May it be Eleazar, and may his offspring be, for thus is the comfort of the people. There is no one for man but the Unique One; in Him there is no grief or sickness. May your prayer be turned towards the shore of His presence.

<meta>This page marks a significant linguistic and thematic shift from the previous page's Samaritan liturgical content. We are now reading a Syriac ascetic treatise, likely from a monastic context, focusing on the "spirituality of the heart." The text emphasizes the "Qyāma" or "standing" in prayer, a central concept in East and West Syriac traditions.</meta>

### On the Standing of Prayer

You, when you stand in your prayer, first of all, recognize the One before whom you stand in prayer, so that the <term>awe of God</term> <note>original: "deḥltā d-alāhā". While often translated as "fear," in this context it refers to the profound reverential dread or wonder felt in the presence of the Divine.</note> may be in your heart. And after this, pray a prayer that has the likeness of the <term>standing of the angels</term> <note>The "angelic life" or "angelic standing" was a common goal in Syriac Christianity, where the monk or practitioner seeks to emulate the perpetual, undistracted worship of the heavenly hosts.</note>.

That is to say, master this act of prayer: every hour that you stand in prayer, believe in the posture <note>original: "qyāmā", literally "standing" or "resurrection." Here it refers to the formal physical and spiritual stance taken during liturgy.</note> that you are standing before God. You, from the spirit—amen—have no reason to move from your place, neither in your body nor in your mind; rather, stand in the awe of God with every limb of your body. Remember every one of your members at that hour when you have a standing before God.

Liken yourself to the angels in the midst of every hour of prayer, lest a single desire turn you aside from your place, or you be disturbed by <term>common thoughts</term> <note>original: "hedyōṭūtā d-ḥušābē". This refers to "idiotic" or "lay" thoughts—mundane, worldly distractions that pull the mind away from the sacred.</note> at that hour of prayer. Pray in peace with the two-fold will of spirit and body in a single mind.

Just as for the standing—when you are lifted up from your place, that you might descend below from your place—at that hour, pray in the awe of God. For God is entirely in all places; but you, acknowledge Him before God from the <term>center of your heart</term> <note>The "middle" or "center" of the heart is a technical term in Syriac mysticism for the point of contact between the human soul and the Divine presence.</note> from the hour of your single standing. Lest in your mind you be <term>inclined toward the city</term> <note>original: "ṣelyā d-mdīnatā". This likely serves as a metaphor for the noise and distractions of worldly society, contrasting the "city" with the "cell" or the focused heart.</note> by a beloved desire, away from the thoughts of prayer from the center of your heart from the hour of prayer—since God knows it all from the center of your heart.

If, therefore, the prayer does not listen to it—the prayer which you heard in the alertness of mind—you have not escaped from thoughts. Just as God has seen you in that hour of prayer, remember the Holy Spirit who sees you at all times of prayer; rather, pray before the Holy Spirit with a single mind. Lest, when you are standing before the Holy Spirit in prayer, your prayer not be heard by you, because in the alertness of your mind you did not escape from the thoughts from your place which you heard in the prayer.

For God listens to you entirely from the center of your heart, and just as He saw you in the desire that raises up the prayer from the hour of your standing. If you wish to escape from thoughts in prayer, acknowledge Him before whom you stand in prayer: that He is God. And before Him be humbled and pray in the awe of God in the center of your heart and in the alertness of your mind. Let the entire reflection of your mind be in the awe of God at all times of prayer, in that you wish to pray to Christ our Lord.

If you would master this act of prayer with every limb of your body in prayer, remember at all times of prayer God who sees you entirely from the center of your heart. After this, remember the angels who see you at all times of prayer, and pray with a single mind, lest you be disturbed by thoughts. And when you wish to pray to God our Lord, pray in the awe of God at all times of prayer. Amen.

<meta>This page continues the monastic instructions on prayer from the previous page, transitioning from the physical posture of "standing" to the internal state of the soul. It addresses a "Simon Basim" and references "John"—most likely John the Solitary (John of Apamea), a 5th-century Syriac author whose work on the three stages of the spiritual life (somatic, psychic/soulful, and pneumatic/spiritual) was foundational to this tradition.</meta>

Simon Basim: In the time of the wonder of the illustrious <note>likely referring to John the Solitary (John of Apamea), a famous ascetic writer</note> John, those who are slightly stirred by the **prayers of the soul**, of the **new prayers**, or even the **spiritual ones** <note>original: "napišātā." In Syriac mystical tradition, this refers to the 'psychic' level of prayer, belonging to the soul's intermediate stage of development between the bodily and the spiritual.</note>; for the repose in this time—this wondrous time—the abundance of our sins shall be made manifest and known at the end, for the remembrance of that which has no end we are prepared. 

O God, You who exist for those who rejoice in mercy and receive from You: for those who seek mercy seek it in You, and mercy is found from You. 

Yes, my God, do not leave me to be a <unclear>distraction</unclear> so that no part of the sins of the <term>physicians</term> <note>In Syriac literature, "The Physician" (Āsyā) is a common title for Christ, who heals the "wounds" of sin.</note> should be upon me. In You I am a witness, and again a petitioner in the time of prayer; for You, O Lord, are the Petitioner who prays for me <note>A profound mystical concept where God is both the object of prayer and the one prompting the prayer within the heart.</note>, through the beautiful life that lives in You. 

You are mercy through the **Lamb** who is the living God in the place of my soul, so that I may be worthy of this also: as a witness of life. Even if you are a sinful soul, turn back, turn back, turn back again to the remembrance from the time in which you were a son, so that it may not be said to the one who is cast out from the path: "Turn back," in the place where it was said to others: "Mercy, mercy from You." 

To those who are known even in remembrance, I am the living handmaid who enters the **City** <note>The "City" often represents the Heavenly Jerusalem or the state of spiritual perfection.</note> in the unique life, the time of life. And they asked him, wondering at the one who weeps in prayer: "I am in life!" In that time, I found myself full of sins, yet living—those who are wiped clean and live as my Christians—from sins, so that I might live in that time a life of preparation, in the life of true life. 

Sins are like a wonder! The God of the living sinner, as if for the <term>awe-struck</term>, a time of life of wonder, a healer in the time of prayer. Like a certain Jeremiah <note>likely a reference to the Prophet Jeremiah or a monastic contemporary</note>, a living one who belongs to Him—may God grant him life from his sins. Amen. As in the time about which he spoke throughout all generations.

<margin>To the beloved John</margin>

From You, however, the Word was extended, which does not cease; those whose prayer is of the soul, like a prayer from sins. 

Truly, when shall our soul become new? God is new and living in that state also. Just as there, so this also has come. Even those who are "You"—how can it be that the time should not rule over me, to pass away from a brother in the place of something? 

What are you, that you should be wiped away easily? O Petitioner, O Petitioner, turn toward the vision of life! You are among those who live in the **true life**, which is the <term>attainment</term> <note>original: "meṭyāḥā". A technical term for reaching the goal of the spiritual journey.</note> of salvation for them, if we are able to live. And if not, something still lives. Turn back, turn back in the remembrance of our soul, as a likeness, as a mercy. 

Amen.

<vocab>
**Prayer** (ṣlōtā): The central act of the monk; categorized here into soul-led and spiritual levels.
**God** (alāhā): The source of mercy and the "Physician" of the soul.
**Sins** (ḥṭāhē): Described as wounds to be healed or obstacles to be wiped away.
**Soul** (napšā): The seat of the intermediate level of prayer and transformation.
**John** (yuḥanān): Likely John of Apamea, whose writings defined these stages of prayer.
**Mercy** (ḥnānā): The primary quality sought by the practitioner from the Divine.
**Remembrance** (dukrānā): Not just memory, but a meditative state of keeping God in mind.
</vocab>

->**With the help of God!**<-

Just as the lives of men are in this present time, there is a yearning from all the worlds that flows like a fountain. For the time that exists is flowing, and everything within it moves like rivers. From father to son and from father to child, the time which is the **love of God** <note>The author views time not as a void, but as a medium filled with Divine Love.</note> is like a fountain from our hands. In the one Name of God it exists; for when life emerges from their yearning, all lives flow toward Him. In their hands is life, which truly is life, and let there be for them the **breath of prayer** <note>original: "nešamtā d-ṣelutā." This suggests that prayer is as vital and natural to the monk as breathing.</note> in every hour and at all times. If indeed at all times they are complete in this, following the **service** <note>original: "šemšā," which can mean service, ministry, or the movement of the sun.</note> of the two prayers by day and by night, as well as the other holy prayers along with the **prayer of the hours**.

The beginning of the time of the service for each of these occurs when one hears the sound of the **horn** <note>original: "qarnā." In monastic communities, a horn, wooden clapper (simandro), or bell was used to call the brothers to prayer.</note>, which comes from the Lord and is in its proper first time. 

The saying of the prayer: 
* At the time of the **Third Hour** <note>9:00 AM. Traditionally associated with the descent of the Holy Spirit.</note>, he says one prayer and another prayer, followed by a hymn and the service of prayer.
* And its last is as its first: another prayer, the prayer of the day and of the night.
* The life of these two types of prayers: the **prayers of the morning** <note>original: "ṣaprā," similar to Lauds or Matins.</note> and the prayer of the **Ninth Hour** <note>3:00 PM. Traditionally associated with the crucifixion and death of Christ.</note>. 
* Also, the prayer of the **evening** <note>Vespers.</note> and the morning.

Let these prayers ascend at every time and hour to the Lord, and let life come from them. Let those who are distressed stand at all the hours of prayer in this world and in the world to come, for there a reward shall be given to them from the Lord; because of their devotion, they are chosen by the Lord. This shall stand there before the <term>Bema</term> <note>The "Bema" is the judgment seat or the raised platform in a church; here it refers to the tribunal of Christ at the final judgment.</note> of judgment. Because then, that which was theirs shall be for them: the morning time, the time of the **Third**, the time of the **Sixth** <note>12:00 PM (Noon).</note>, and the time of the **Ninth**. 

And at evening, and again the service of the night, for therein are served all the **purifications** <note>original: "tazkyātā" - acts of making pure or being declared innocent/acquitted of sin.</note> of man. In these times, they shall call them the "times of prayer," and the prayers are in their hands so that they may see all the purifications of the sins within them. For these prayers are what bring them near, just as in the time of the Third, it is as one day. 

Here, while in the body, even their masters who appear in their time shall be present. At the time of the Ninth, they shall complete the **psalms of the night** and the prayers of the morning. Because there shall be "breaths" for me from the Lord for those who serve in this time, being chosen for the Lord. Righteousness and holiness shall not depart from them forever. 

Amen.

<term>Asuta (Healing)</term> — In Mandaean tradition, this is not just physical health but a spiritual wholeness and protection from demonic influence.
<term>Zakuta (Victory)</term> — The successful outcome of a soul's journey or a ritual's efficacy; a state of being "vindicated" by the Light.

<meta>While the previous page focused on a protective amulet (the Letter of Bar Zebdai), this page shifts abruptly into a dense theological treatise concerning Christology and the Incarnation. It appears to be a fragment of a commentary or a translation of a Patristic text—likely related to Irenaeus of Lyons, as mentioned in a marginal gloss—discussing the mystery of the Word (Logos) taking on flesh and the subsequent victory over death.</meta>

# Concerning the Time Given in Advance

Listening is not merely a single path, or rather, the "obstacle of the hands" <note>This likely refers to human limitations or the physical constraints of the created world</note> which is managed against God—who does not exist within time from the beginning of creation. Nor is the physical wonder <note>a reference to the miracle of the Incarnation</note> concerning the descent of the Word <term>Miltha: The Divine Word or Logos, the second person of the Trinity</term> who is from God. It was fitting that the destruction of the body should be made divine. Because something of it is known and visible, when it is desired through teaching, it is not rejected but rather flies toward heaven, even when it sees that the body is built up in honor; or when it is seen to give life from what is not expected in this parched life.
<gloss>Or: The revelation after the resurrection</gloss>

The body, which is the world of immortality, has had its destruction removed. The destruction of the body—which even in the graves shall live—[occurs through] the methods by which he ceases from being weeds to become the trees of righteousness. It is right for this to be unique in the world, stemming from the joy that came to be,
<gloss>Or: Because in Him we are saved</gloss>

to the knowledgeable through the life of [the Spirit]. But as if he were accompanied by the hands of the disciple to signal to him from the state of mortality, it was made known to us that the Will, like a quiet and incorruptible genus, [acted] through the holy woman <note>A traditional reference to the Virgin Mary, the Theotokos</note> who gave birth to God the Word. The body was given to all through the Giver of Life <note>original: "Mhayya," a common Syriac title for Christ</note> over death, from the acts of redemption which He laid hold of even in death itself. Also, the quiet life—which is pure and unchosen—is seen in the brightness that descended. Creation was justified when it was made known from the "Place of Life."

And to you also, through the sweetness of the Word of the Giver of Life—in the life which is not Christ <note>This may be a polemical point or a textual corruption; it likely means "not merely a human Christ"</note> who is built up as the Life-giver—it is known that through the quickening of the body which was given, even in time, a sealed soul exists within it. When the way of the Law was washed, as it was desired for him, the Godhead was humbled/brought low by the hands of the Word of God, just as was seen to be right and fitting. What wonder appeared from this?
<gloss>(Or: Irenaeus)</gloss> <note>A reference to Irenaeus of Lyons (c. 130–202 AD), an early Church Father known for his writings on the Incarnation</note>

The thought of life was opened like the divisions of the soul, which comes from the power of victory. From this, even time follows it, and the vision [is] that he shall consume the parched place. Regarding the number of the salvation, therefore, the soul which was breathed in belongs to Him. From the quickening, it is not its own, but its birth is from God the Word, for those who [inherit] the kingdom through the Will. 

Where is the dominion? In whose hand are we? The destruction of the body was against that which was in life. And the Word of God—wherever the holy place is, wherever the soul goes out—belongs to the body which is right, according to the vision of the Life-giver of the resurrection of God. The quickening of the Word, which was humbled for the victory of Christ, is like the redemption of the quickening of the Word. Do not wonder at this: it is not against what was previously seen of the Will of God. For you are the earth that gives life in it; conversely, He gives life upon the ground <note>original: "Adim," referring to the surface of the earth or the ground from which Adam was formed</note> because He willed every vision. For when man is a child of heaven, where then is the salvation? Where?

<summary>A theological meditation on the Incarnation and Resurrection, explaining how the "Word of God" (Miltha) descended to assume a mortal body and overcome death. The text references the Virgin Mary, the role of Christ as the Giver of Life, and cites the authority of Irenaeus.</summary>

# Concerning the Five Chapters and the Meal Offerings

And when you follow the path <note>original: "al-sabil," referring to the correct legal or spiritual way</note> regarding the **Five Chapters**, five scholars <term>Ulama: Learned teachers or religious authorities</term> have gathered concerning the community of the faith and the state of the lands. And the interpretation is: if one of them requires a supplement from the others, gathered... and if they spoke the tradition <note>original: "al-sunnah," meaning the established law or practice</note>.

O God, the Merciful, the Gracious, turn Your face and Your favor toward us, and turn away from the heat of Your anger.

**When you prepare (for dressing)**: in total, the bond of good speech in His nostrils <note>A common Samaritan liturgical idiom referring to God's "nose" or "nostrils" cooling from anger into favor</note> and You shall return to His people in God. **When you prepare with the letter Aleph (for journeying)**: in total, returning in all this, O Merciful One, in His nostrils and You shall return to His people in God. Comforting in the Name, with weeping and Your favor in its fullness. Grace be upon you and upon all.

**When you prepare by hand (for lodging)**: in total, and returning in the good Name from her labor in His nostrils and You shall return to His people in God. All that is to come and the help of Your pious ones, quickly, with weeping and Your favor in its fullness, O Gracious and Merciful One.

<meta>This page continues the liturgical or talismanic instructions from page 72, shifting from specific bodily preparations to a liturgical calendar. The soul of us all is the death of one whose soul is pleasant; and the lamb of the righteous is joyful, gladdening the souls of certain people among her offspring.

**The Month of Adar (The Fish)** <note>original: "Bar Huta" — Pisces</note> as the Son of the East <note>A common title for a scholar or authority from the Eastern regions of the Syriac-speaking world</note> said: In the month of Adar, the soul of us all shall be gathered in a certain manner; a certain report of the righteous is heard today, on that day when the gift is promised, for which we give thanks to Him.

**The Month of Nisan (The Lamb)** <note>original: "Emra" — Aries</note> as the Son of the District said: In Hebrew, the month of Nisan is another kind [of time]. He prepared Nisan to see joy in it, because as of today, there is redemption from Egypt, and a separation from the Egyptians.

**The Month of Iyar (The Bull)** <note>original: "Bar Tawra" — Taurus</note> as the triumphant Singer <note>Likely referring to King David</note> said: In the month of Iyar, let us praise God. The soul of us all rejoices that we went out from Egypt and are coming toward Paradise, because as of today, we departed from Egypt.

**The Month of Sivan (The Twins)** <note>original: "Bar Tame" — Gemini</note> as the Son of the Syrian said: In the month of Sivan, the soul of us all is thirsty to give the soul a drink. A certain manner is conducted by the Teacher <note>original: "Mushlamana" — often used for one who transmits tradition</note> today, because [God] gave the Law to Moses on **Mount Sinai**.

**The Month of Tammuz (The Crab)** <note>original: "Bar Sartana" — Cancer</note> as the Son of the Himiite said: In the month of Tammuz, the soul of us all fears the parching heat. A certain manner [of grief] from the destruction of the weak today is released, because of the sin of the breaking of the Tablets <note>A reference to the Golden Calf incident when Moses broke the stone tablets of the Ten Commandments</note>.

**The Month of Ab (The Lion)** <note>original: "Bar Aryo" — Leo</note> as the Son of the Median said: In the month of Ab, the soul of us all is the measure of the Father; and the Father who is over the Governor performed it. In it today, something of death comes with him, and he cares for that day.

**The Month of Elul (The Ear of Grain)** <note>original: "Bar Shebola" — Virgo</note> as the Son of the Elamite said: In the month of Elul, let the soul of us all put on peace. Regarding the word of the departure of the Lamb <note>original: "Emra" — could refer to the Zodiac sign or a sacrificial lamb</note>, his departure is on this very day, and in it, victory is sealed.

**The Month of Ethanim (The Scales)** <note>original: "Bar Masata" — Libra. Ethanim is the ancient name for the month of Tishrei</note> as the Son of the Tayyite <note>Referring to the Tayy tribe, or more broadly to an Arab authority</note> said: In the month of Ethanim, the soul of us all shall journey to see the height of Zion, which is the city. In it, he gave the Tabernacle of service, and a manner of life.

**The Month of Bul (The Scorpion)** <note>original: "Bar 'Aqrqba" — Scorpio. Bul is the ancient name for the month of Heshvan</note> as the Son of the Mimiite said: In the month of Bul, the soul of us all is redeemed. In the coming of the fatness <note>symbolizing prosperity or blessing</note> that we might see; in it today, in the renowned fulfillment, a manner of life is within it.

**The Month of Kislev (The Archer)** <note>original: "Bar Qashta" — Sagittarius</note> as the Son of the East said: In the month of Kislev, the soul of us all waits...

**The Month of Tebeth (The Kid)** <note>original: "Bar Gadya" — Capricorn</note> as the Son of the East said: In the month of Tebeth, the soul of us all sees...

<meta>This page transitions from the liturgical calendar and zodiacal associations seen on page 73 into a theological litany or a "hymn of being." While the previous page focused on the movement of time and historical redemption (Egypt/Exodus), this text focuses on the eternal nature of God and His relationship to various ranks of the celestial and terrestrial hierarchy (Angels, Prophets, Martyrs). It emphasizes the concept of "Ithutha" (Being or Essence) as the source of all sanctification.</meta>

In this vessel <note>original: "qlita" — possibly referring to a prayer-vessel, a small chamber, or a spiritual treasury</note> which Christ, the Son of God, gave as a name...

<margin>1</margin> **Amen, Amen.** (In that He is) This one who stands before you, I say to you, He is the Exalted God; and to Him all the worlds, together with all the powers that are in this dark region, serve His <term>Essence</term> <note>Syriac: "Ithutha" — a technical term in Syriac theology referring to the fundamental nature or "being-ness" of God</note> from before the worlds. Amen.

<margin>2</margin> **The God of Truth.** (In that He is) He who is from the world without time; and time is not felt in Him, nor is there fear. Regarding His Essence: the life that we possess is in this, His Essence; let that life of His Essence be from the external life <note>A distinction between the self-existent life of God and the life granted to creatures</note>.

<margin>3</margin> **The God of the Saints.** (In that He is) From His Essence all the saints are sanctified. In that Essence, He created every person who comes into the world. By this Essence, He created time and the worlds, and all living things are beautifully fashioned from His Essence.

---

<margin>4</margin> **The God of the Angels.** (In that He is) From Him, all the angels are known, who are in the sweet place of life <note>A poetic Syriac idiom for Paradise or the celestial realm</note>; for all the angels served Him in every form of the ministry of His Essence, according to His will.

<margin>5</margin> **The God of the Spirits.** (In that He is) From Him, the rational spirits are known, those of the region in the sweet place of life; they serve the Life of lives which flows from His Essence. Amen. The Spirit also goes forth alone from His Essence and is beautifully guided.

---

<margin>6</margin> **The God of the Prophets.** (In that He is) From His Essence all the prophets speak, those who are in the place of life; they are heard from Him. From His Essence, prophecy also goes forth, and from His Essence it is made visible.

<margin>7</margin> **The God of the Apostles.** (In that He is) For they all belong to Him; from His Essence even all the apostles are [sent], those who are in the sweet place of life. Also from His Essence, apostleship goes forth according to its command.

<margin>8</margin> **The God of the Martyrs.** (In that He is) From His Essence all the martyrs are offered, those who are in the sweet place of life. From Him they are offered at all times in the place of life, in which they are beautifully preserved by the life of His Essence. From His Essence, martyrdom <note>Literally "witness"</note> also goes forth.

<margin>9</margin> **The God of the Confessors.** (In that He is) From Him all the confessors <note>Those who suffered for the faith but were not killed</note> are known, those who are in the sweet place of life.

<margin>10</margin> **The God of the Righteous.** (In that He is) From Him all the righteous are known, those who are in the sweet place of life. 
Amen.

<summary>A liturgical litany declaring God as the source of all existence (Essence), and the master of the various ranks of the holy, from angels and spirits to prophets and martyrs.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the transition from the previous discussion on the mystical "Seal of Noah" and holy names. # In the name of the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit, one God, Amen.

**The King** <note>original: "Melka" — referring here to God as the Sovereign Ruler of the celestial hierarchy</note> in whose name the angels are seen and whom they praise. The Alpha <note>original: "Alpa" — likely referring to the primary principle or the first sequence of a mystical ritual</note> of the third day, in which the world was created, is called "the World of Births." Something is called "third," the Alpha of the third day, because on the third day the waters were gathered into one place and the dry land appeared. And because the third is the third day in which the waters were gathered, for its portion <note>original: "manwa" — a share or designated part</note>, a voice <term>original: "qala" — in Syriac tradition, this refers to a liturgical mode or a specific rhythmic chant/sound used in prayer</term> sounds when the King is praised in holiness at all times, Amen. 

And after the King speaks, the third voice shall sound, which pertains to the third day: the gathering of the sea away from the dry land, and also the herbs and trees that came forth from it. The third Alpha is the King whom the angels praise. Primarily, the third voice is called the Alpha of the third day, in which the waters were gathered and also <margin>also trees</margin> herbs and trees came forth from the earth. And also the waters were gathered in one place and the dry land appeared. 

And because of this, we say "The King is praised in holiness" in this third voice, in which the waters were gathered. After the third voice is spoken, when the King is invoked <note>original: "naqesh" — literally "to strike," likely referring to the ringing of a prayer bell (naqusha) or the rhythmic beginning of the chant</note> on the third day, if a person desires that their prayer might stand <note>meaning to be established, accepted, or effective</note> in the night and also in the morning, they shall say this voice which is the fifth, so that it may be as on the fifth day. Because on this fifth day, the fish and the birds were created from the waters. 

And because of this, the prayer in this fifth voice is full of praise; one says it when their prayer is invoked in the night and also in the morning of the Sabbath <note>original: "shabta" — can refer specifically to Saturday or the week in general; here it likely refers to the liturgical preparation for the Sabbath</note> instead of the day in which the waters were gathered. Because of this, the prayer is in this fifth voice in which the fish and birds were created from the waters, as it is said.

**The King** whom the angels praise. Primarily, the fifth voice is called the Alpha of the fifth day, in which the fish and birds were created from the waters. And because of this, the prayer in this fifth voice—in which the fish and birds were created from the waters—is said on the fifth day.

**The King** whom the angels praise. Primarily, the sixth voice is called the Alpha of the sixth day, in which the animals, the creeping things, and the likeness of Adam were created from the earth. Because of this, the prayer is in this sixth voice, because Adam was created from the earth in it.

# Page 57: The Person while Living 57

### # Blessed is he in whom God dwells

As it is written concerning faith in our Lord and Savior **Jesus Christ**, who granted to humanity the path to become His children and heirs of His kingdom. He has also made us—sinners and wretched as we are—worthy to be counted among those people who dwell <note>This refers to the "dwellers of the desert," the monks and hermits</note> in His presence. He has deemed us worthy so that we too might be joined to the suffering of our Lord Christ and His holy Resurrection from the house of the dead. May we be made worthy of those leaders <term>Leaders: From the Syriac 'mdabrone', referring to spiritual directors or the Desert Fathers who "guided" the souls of monks</term> who led the way through discipline, fasting, prayer, and the beautiful conduct of the saints who knew our Lord, the Master of all the worlds. Our Christ.

-> Again, one [thing] <-

Let us also recognize these guides who were themselves human beings of our own flesh and blood. In what way shall we follow the blessed righteous one and famous teacher, **Mar Isaac of Nineveh**? <note>Saint Isaac of Nineveh (c. 613–700 AD), also known as Isaac the Syrian, was a highly influential bishop and theologian known for his writings on Christian asceticism and mysticism.</note> For he also traveled along this same guiding path; a true guide of life who conducted himself as if he were in heaven. He warned all his children, and even us—human guides—concerning their own weakness, so that they might receive strength from his teaching according to their ability—both the monks living in monasteries and those seeking quietude in the meadows <note>original: "margo" — often refers to a lush or quiet place of retreat</note>, for the guiding path belongs to all of us. And whoever follows the teaching of this guide, even as one coming from the world, let them see how they are led. As the wonderful teacher **Mar Isaac** said: even in the wilderness of the mountains, those who have come from the desert, if they fulfill the practices he described to them while walking that path, they shall bring joy to the King. **Mar Isaac** is a guide who leads them in the faith given to them by the living and eternal God, for in these things they live and shall find life—even in stillness <term>Stillness: 'Shelyo', a central concept in Syriac mysticism referring to an inner and outer silence that allows the soul to experience God</term>—just as those who knew the Lord of all worlds did. Amen.

-> Again, the Truth <-

The King [comes] from stillness, just as he knew our Lord, for the guiding path comes through faith in the Christ who saved the worlds. Along this path, they live with all their soul, all their mind, and all their strength. As the teacher and guide **Mar Isaac** said through the Holy Spirit: let no one tell those people who are led in the wilderness that they are not guided by faith. This vision of the one who saw the truth in the wilderness is blessed; for in the wilderness of fasting, the one who saw it was guided by the prayer of the saints, according to the strength they possessed. Amen and Amen. And from the guide, through all the years, there is water from the fruits which the teacher shall receive—just as it is spoken in the writings of the most holy one. Amen and Amen.

-> Again <-

May the King of Stillness, on the day of our Lord’s coming, find the teacher and the guide standing before Him in the God of Christ. Amen.

# Seal of the Ruler

<note>original Syriac: "shaliṭā." This refers to a person of authority or a ruling spiritual entity.</note>

This seal is to be held in one's hand so that everything may be turned toward him by the holy ones <note>original: "ܩܕܝܫܐ" (qadishe), which can refer to saints or, more likely in this context, angelic beings</note>, along with the ritual administration of this seal.

### This Parable Concerns This Moral Instruction

This concerns anyone who thinks in his heart to involve other people in his deeds—the "work of man"—so that they might say he possesses virtuous qualities, and they might praise them and say "Blessed be God" <note>meaning, people who perform good deeds just to receive public recognition of their piety</note>. Such a person is like the noble insect called a spider (<gloss>Aranea</gloss> <note>Latin/Spanish for spider</note>) which spins and weaves a fabric of spiderwebs to catch flies smaller than itself among buildings and structures. Spiderwebs are by nature weak; even if the insect is small compared to a fly, if the webs take hold, they cannot actually hold a creature that is larger than the web itself. 

So it is, and more so, for one who hangs his trust upon the thoughts he displays before other people, whether in public or in secret. <term>The Holy One, Blessed be He</term> <note>original: HaKadosh Baruch Hu—a standard Hebrew title for God</note> gazes into the hearts of man and not at what the human eyes see. A man's thoughts cannot be hidden, and his soul will be successful without a doubt if it faces a straight <term>Providence</term> <note>original: Hashgacha—the concept of Divine supervision or intervention in the world</note>, for he trusts in Him in truth. From all this, we learn that one should not abandon his providence and his thoughts to anything but the Heavens, to <term>The Lord, The Lord</term> <note>original: YY' YY'—a scribal abbreviation for the four-letter Name of God</note>. And in the time of your trust, they shall reach him; through your trust and your hope—a hope that rises like the sand of the sea—he shall attain His mercies upon him, making the path successful to understand the temperament of the soul.

<meta>catchword: And they (והם)</meta>

<summary>This page discusses the spiritual necessity of wholehearted trust in God (Bitachon), emphasizing that Divine help depends on the sincerity of one's heart rather than outward displays of piety. It uses the "Parable of the Spider" to illustrate how hypocritical or superficial faith is as flimsy and ineffective as a spider's web.</summary>
<keywords>Trust in God, Divine Providence, Bitachon, Hashgacha, Spider's Web Parable, Sincerity, Miracles, Morality</keywords>

The manner which is in the powers of the Distinct One and time, as it was said in the beginning; and to the Distinct One who was known from his word, he said to her—as to these times in the beginning, in the creation which was in it—even if they are distinguished according to the **person of the kind** <note>original: parṣopā d-znā — a personified category or type</note>, as we might see.

Also, to one Distinct One of the power from which creation comes, he said to her in this time of its children: through signs he shows them the manner of the power which is these four times. They are four times of the power that is found after the first Distinct One.

In this he said they are found as **intermediaries** <note>original: meṣyātā — means, instruments, or middle-things</note>, and they are four times of power that are found after the Distinct One of this time. From this which he said in something like... through signs, as it was given to her through signs of time, as it is fitting from all of them in the time given to her, as that change of the Divine Word is in this, according to the manner...

->With the help of Heaven<-

# The Book of the Law of Man
<note>original: *Sefer Torat HaAdam*—A major work by the 13th-century scholar Rabbi Moses ben Nahman (Nahmanides or Ramban). While much of the book focuses on mourning laws, this section deals with the "Joy of the Groom."</note>

What our teacher, of blessed memory <note>original: *zlah* — "May his memory be for the life of the world to come"</note>, wrote regarding the arrangement of the blessings for grooms and those similar to them, and the betrothal blessing <note>original: *birkat erusin*</note>
which includes these blessings, and the wedding blessing which is everywhere called the **Seven Blessings** <note>original: *sheva brachot*</note>. We have explained
many things about them in our book in the appropriate place, and they are a commandment <note>original: *mitzvah*</note> from the **words of the Scribes** <note>original: *divrei soferim* — referring to Rabbinic law rather than biblical law</note>. And for every commandment
that involves a physical action and has a fixed time, one says the blessing at the time of its performance. Regarding these Seven Blessings:
at the time of the wedding canopy <note>original: *chuppah*</note>, one should stand and bless, because the essence of these Seven Blessings belongs in the house of the grooms,
and we bless the grooms with them for the sake of the **joy** <note>original: *simcha*</note>. From the time of the wedding canopy and throughout the days of the feast,
this joy is not invalidated. Every time that **new faces** <note>original: *panim hadashot* — guests who were not present at previous meals</note> arrive during all seven days of the wedding canopy, one recites
the Seven Blessings, as Rav Yehuda said: "Rav said: One recites the Seven Blessings all seven days of
the feast every day that there is joy there in the house and 'new faces' have arrived." And this is the reason
that throughout the seven days of joy, we bless them in the house of the grooms and not in the **synagogue** <note>original: *beit ha-knesset*</note> unless
they lead the groom there. And they did not need to specify this, for the "house of the groom" is simply wherever
the wedding canopy and the meal are located. It may be said that the joy is primarily in the house of the grooms; therefore

Clarification
</insert>
</margin>
our Sages, of blessed memory, said: We bless at the wedding canopy <note>In the house of the groom, specifically in the place of joy</note>
and all the days of joy we bless at his table and all through the seven days of the feast,
since the primary joy is found in eating and drinking. And such is the law in the synagogue: if they bring
grooms there and "new faces," they should bless them there, because the synagogue is a place to which joy belongs,
and furthermore, because there all Israel is uplifted and their fortune <note>original: *mazalam*</note> is improved by hearing the world. Therefore, they have practiced this in the **Diaspora** <note>original: *gola*</note>
as we have said: in the house of the grooms and around the table. And in their time, they would bless the order of the wedding canopy, and I have practiced
this way at the time of the canopy: one man, in a place where there are "new faces," recites the **Seven Blessings** <note>original: *yb"sh* — abbreviation for *sheva brachot*</note> and arranges them,
since this is a "house." And our Sages taught: "on every day," as we explained there. And so we have practiced from the ancients to stand
at the meal and other feasts: in this case where "new faces" have arrived and will arrive, and he blesses for them and arranges
the end of these in his house, except for a single individual. And he should bless the Grace After Meals there and the Groom's Blessings in the
proper order. Regarding the canopy and the blessings in the house of the groom, one does not bless and is not obligated in the custom on the Sabbaths
of the wedding canopy.

# A Universal Practice for Success and Favor

<margin>
This script belongs to the sign that is effective for all things.
</margin>

One method among the things that are beneficial for every matter, applicable to everything, and for finding **favor**:
Whoever draws this script should write it upon parchment. After writing it, he should dip it in **honey** and **rose water**. By the name of the Teacher <note>Likely referring to a divine or angelic intelligence that grants insight</note> who provides understanding, one should taste a little of it and hold it in his hands. He should then smoke it lightly with **fine incense** over the coals while it is in his hands. If it is within a book, write it using the first letters of the words and pass over it with the honey we mentioned. When he goes among other people, if it is in his hands or his name is upon it, he will always be **successful** in all his handiwork; he will be a leader and will benefit on that day from protection against any **evil eye** <note>The "evil eye" refers to a traditional belief in a curse cast by a malevolent or envious glare</note> from an enemy's heart.

YHVH

<note>This is the "wing" or "shield" formation of the Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of God. In Kabbalistic practice, writing the name progressively is believed to draw divine energy down into the physical world.</note>

Furthermore, anyone who writes this **drawn sign**—which is called the "Sign for Matters"—and keeps it with him in a clean place or binds it to his **right arm**, its <term>virtue</term> <note>original: "segullah," a spiritual property, remedy, or supernatural power inherent in an object or action</note> is such that you will succeed through it in whatever you ask for: **favor and grace**, receiving good gifts, and kindness. All his desires will be fulfilled by his hand, and he shall always have trust in the Creator.

He answered and said to him, offering him a fine greeting, and through his news and service, he kissed his hands and returned him to his seat. He kissed his hands and said regarding it: "The leading men of your land are [intent] upon generosity in a single grasp; my hand is with a book, and another is with modesty and goodness." <unclear>He said</unclear>: "And for all who strive against him with envy and evil, [they are] low and abject, and every word of evil [is silenced], while he appears with goodness in all the world. --- Page 110 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the courtly narrative of the previous page—which described a gathering of wise men and the "Sultan of Women"—into a dense philosophical and cosmological treatise. The text shifts focus to the "Active Intellect" and the soul's journey through celestial spheres, justifying the "perfection" promised by the book mentioned previously.</meta>

In all the deeds of **His Great Excellency** <note>Hebrew abbreviation "HKM": likely *Ha-Kavod Ma'alato*, a formal title of respect</note>, we knew [him] <gloss>to sit</gloss> in the assembly of the wise and the testers, so that they might write for him concerning the one matter who walks in these paths. He shall grant him some standing among them, showing what is worthy, correct, and straight. From this will come these deeds and the other paths; and this is the designated way to uphold him, and not by his walking generally [or haphazardly] through the mixtures of the **spheres** <note>the celestial spheres that medieval thinkers believed governed the natural world</note> and the soul. For the soul is intertwined with the power of capability.

For every man, in his spirit, is compelled to act and to journey, for they have no [other choice]. These desires—each of them according to its own nature—will emerge in his walking. He will see in them his lack of perfection, whether in his intellect or in the love of his body, rather than in his following and being inflamed toward that which is above—which is the source and the form of the body. 

The body is the opposite of this; and in the opposite, everything will fail to attain the **Active Intellect** <note>original: "Sekhel ha-Po'el," the bridge between the human mind and the Divine in medieval Aristotelian philosophy</note>. And behold, in this person, there will be some change as he turns, and then he comes into a lack of intellect and a lack of strength, and his walking will be filled [with difficulty]. Through the power of the moving sense, which is intertwined with the **spheres of the generation** and the soul, he cannot endure the **angels** in what appears to them in their **spirituality**, which is what is imprinted within them. Whether one enjoys them or finds them bitter, the soul is established as divided; it is not a simple substance, except for what it was in its connection to the intellect that activates it. By that intellect, she is helped, and in this way, she will be joined to that same intellect, for this capability is not divided: rather, it is like the **Moving Intellect**. 

And behold, in all the ways a man may choose, he will find that **Moving Intellect** there, in conjunction with these powers, as we have said in all places. In whichever of these paths where a weak and divided necessity is not found, they shall grant him a weak and yet weaker intellect; for their action concerns the matters of the lower beings which appear to them. They believe they are giving him power according to the weakness of their own knowledge. Indeed, it is **intelligible** <note>original: "muskal," an object of intellectual perception</note> to all that the **abundance** <note>original: "shefa," the divine emanation or flow of wisdom</note> that was upon them was not of their own high standing.

And from here comes the death of those who walk [these paths], namely those who were not careful regarding the life of the soul. Therefore, he will be devoid of **success** <note>original: "hatzlacha," which in this context refers to spiritual bliss or the soul's ultimate achievement</note> because it is not known to them in detail. This results from his lack of contemplation in this book, through which perfection will come to him in the narrative. This is the "intertwined level," and it is the drawing out of the soul.

**Servant** <note>catchword: "Eved"</note>

<meta>This page continues the philosophical exploration of the "Active Intellect" from the previous page, shifting into a Christological and cosmological discourse. First, we should distinguish how—and by what means—all things in this time exist. Since they are known through [the senses], they desire that which is heard beautifully concerning them in this discourse. If the one who preceded him exists, then it is for him—and even more so for the one who commanded it. Yet at other times, the demonstration of that time in which he was first guided does not seem [right] to them. From whom would you distinguish one from the other? 

Even when they see that **Light** <note>Aramaic: *nehira*</note> comes to him in that time, they also know that they exist in a constant, beautiful light to send his will. We have found that you [are not alone], even when every **Wise One** <note>Aramaic: *khakima*</note> knows that he has authority to become a teacher to those whom he makes wise in this matter. For when he sees a sign of the light from men, which sends forth the light that is fitting for him to distinguish, he does not lead [blindly]. It is known that the **Demonstration** <note>Aramaic: *aplaqta*, likely from the Greek *apodeixis*, meaning a logical proof</note> of him who commanded is right for us to distinguish, just as it was distinguished for him.

---

Again, through this, all of them immediately know the **Revelation** <note>Aramaic: *gelyana*</note> to those who see them. Even as they see them, another does not see them except as if they were many—though the **Glory** <note>Aramaic: *teshbukhta*</note> of those who see them is as if it were according to how they see him who descended from that which they saw. Since we have found that those who understand also [bring] peace to those who see them through the **Word** <note>Aramaic: *meltha*, referring to the Divine Logos</note> that is in it. 

For in this, behold, it comes because the Word is complete in this, to say that he is able to be believed. While he exists, he knows that he was not solitary, for one of them at other times is known as the **Only-Begotten** <note>Aramaic: *yakhida* and *yalida*, traditional titles for Christ as the 'born' or 'only' son</note>. Where is it possible for destruction to occur upon that which is right? For him, discernment is not beautiful, unless he receives [the truth]. Not to say that he commanded all these thousand **Parasangs** <note>Aramaic: *parsayya*, a Persian unit of distance used here metaphorically to denote a vast expanse of space or time</note> of time, or that I should fear even him himself. When he sees all—that if I am born of him who commanded—what is right is struck <note>Aramaic: *methnaqash*, meaning to be struck or to ring out, like a bell or a signal</note>. And where does he say in it? **"The Word was"** <note>Aramaic: *meltha ith hawath*; a direct reference to the opening of the Gospel of John: "In the beginning was the Word"</note>.

---

And he who hears all this which he commanded—to be born, which he commanded in time—is like one who waits in his divided power. By the hand of each one of them, they shall see them; since they are of those whom you saw, he repeats to them another [story]. Thus he waits for what is right for him. When he sees them, that which is right is struck, so that he may **rejoice in power**.

--- Page 119 ---
<meta>This page continues the philosophical exploration of the "Degrees of Prophecy," transitioning from the mystical celestial influences of the previous section to a more structured, scholastic analysis of how divine light interacts with the soul. The script is a Judeo-Arabic cursive, and the content appears to be a commentary on Maimonides' classification of prophecy, though it extends to a "12th Degree," which is unusual in standard Maimonidean thought.</meta>

After we have mentioned the adjustment and the causal explanation <note>original: *al-ta'lil*, a technical term in medieval philosophy for providing a rational cause or explanation</note> and his words of thanks for what we described regarding the one being praised <note>likely referring to Moses as the "praised one" of all prophets</note>, know that after something was completed at the end of **from the Second Degree**, the distinction of the fifth degree becomes clear in its conclusion. This concerns the custom regarding the light that used to descend upon him and which God would show him... at the conclusion of the light that Moses <note>original: *Al-Moshe*</note> used to see. He mentioned it continually as if in the sun, being guided and choosing what was for him in its continuity.

And the Lord <note>original: *YHVH*</note> gives him life and buries him, and his memory shall endure in this... and from his mention in the species and the parts. Even if he seeks refuge in God and the silence <note>original: *dumiyah*</note> of his mention, fearing the departure and extraction of Moses, the mention of Moses is manifest in his beginning and his end. And that is in the conclusion of the "garment" <note>a metaphor for the physical or mental vessel that receives divine emanation</note> in the hour of standing and existence. And that is when he mentioned to them that when they mention it, they would become dry... and the mention of Moses and their elevation is apparent, the One and the Nothing. And there was not for them a single thing from his mention except for that which is in the light, and what is in the light from his mention. And he saw his mention only in truth, in that which was hidden from the form of reality and was nullified, even if it were true.

->**From the 11th Degree, Second [Part]**<-

The 11th Degree contains a specific order, and in it, by the light, at a time when the reality which is thought to be a composition <note>original: *tarkib*, referring to a complex or material structure</note> is not the reality that the light brings down to the illuminated soul... and if he mentions and excels in imagination in its description, and it belongs to him in the entire text and the saying of the scholar <note>original: *al-alam*, which can mean "the world" or "the scholar" depending on vowels; in this context, it likely refers to a philosophical authority</note>. 

And in that is his protection, which is not after the cycle was completed and he was salted in the thought of it, in the degree of its cause. In this, there is labor afterwards.
