## The Eighth Degree of Conduct

<margin>This is the Gul <note>original: *Gul*, possibly a shorthand for *Galgal* (Sphere) or a specific mystical term</note> of the degree</margin>
This degree deals with the gathering and assembly of **the Awakeners** <term>Awakeners (Me'orerim): spiritual forces or human initiates who "awaken" divine emanation or communal fervor</term> with the company of **Joshua son of Nun** <note>the successor of Moses and a military leader, symbolizing spiritual strength</note>. And the Awakener, in his stature from the ancient days, provides chosen men for them—strong Awakeners who are experts in war and the instruments of war. Their clothing and tools are polished and broad, and they act within the thick of battle. They stand face to face, sometimes from one side, sometimes from another, and sometimes from the flank. Perhaps deception may be found, and perhaps they will fall in battle despite their might, yet they go out and act and believe like mighty men of valor. There shall be a living camp and an enduring camp. And even if some flee, as if their inner parts were broken, the land and its surroundings shall be glorified before the Lord, the **Messianic Awakener** is his name. In holiness above, they hear the sound of the calling of sacrifice and charity, and of battles and righteousness. They shall anoint their faces (and he is **the Supreme Gul-Gul**) and that which is upon them and from him, to glorify him on the chosen day through the sanctification of oil and fatness. For at the time of battle they are steadfast, and their trust is in the Place <note>original: *Ha-Makom*, a traditional Jewish epithet for God</note> who is praised with a tongue of strength, to show the power of their victory through the law of the appointment of the Awakeners. This is done to kindle trust in the hearts of those who remain, and to project fear and trembling into the inner chambers of the hearts of our enemies. Their throats shall destroy pleasantness <note>perhaps meaning they shout down the false peace of the enemy</note>, or provide a broad space of pleasantness for those who showed my strength and their might. They shall help the Place where their mouths speak the secrets of the **Torah** in his kingdom and in his faith.

## From the Ninth Degree: Aquarius

This degree deals with the gathering in the degree of Awakeners with the might of the *Kamatz* <note>original: *Kamatz*, a Hebrew vowel sign ( ָ ), used here in a kabbalistic or symbolic sense to represent "closure" or "gathering"</note> and the righteousness of the King who lives in life, and the resurrection of the dead, and the coming of the **Messiah** in the midst of the circumcised ones from the sons of Abraham. Israel lives for eternity; and in the days of the Awakener, there is an increase of vitality along with the way and the honor in their speech, their action, and their prayer within the rectifications <term>Rectifications (Tikkunim): spiritual acts intended to repair or perfect the soul and the world</term> of the Fortress. Their hearts are spread out in all their prayers, in which their mouths speak praise in their celebration at every time that the moment is awakened. This shall suffice for him to believe in Him and not to crave after lusts.

### From the Eighth Degree regarding the Torah

The eighth degree is that composed books should come into our hands; they too shall be included here strongly among them. They tell of the passing—peace be upon their resting place—of the Rabbis of our nation in all the lands of the Gentiles. Sometimes their names and their greatness are written in them, from **Adam the First** <note>The biblical first man, traditionally seen as the start of all human history and lineages.</note> until now. They explain in what manner and through what interpretation the words of **our Sages of blessed memory** <note>original: *Hazal*, an acronym for the ancient rabbinic authorities of the Mishnah and Talmud.</note> [are understood]. They assist here and recount at the head of every group the "Order of Generations" <note>original: *Seder Ha-Dorot*. This refers to a genre of Jewish historical literature that chronicles the chain of tradition and the lives of great scholars to establish the legitimacy of their teachings.</note> of a certain person found here or there, as if a person knew how to learn them by heart. What is proper to understand from them are the proofs and the story of these our chronicles <note>original: *divrei yameinu*, literally "the words of our days," often used to mean history or annals.</note>, reflecting visions of internal war <note>This likely refers to spiritual struggles or perhaps historical civil unrest described in these chronicles.</note> toward the ways of study in brief. 

And there are beautiful hints there, even regarding an individual matter from the Gentiles, for the court pays to bequeath their judgment to them. This is not hidden in the later writings, which contain beautiful parables and a pleasant and precious narrative. There are books that are entirely concerned with these matters at length, and even more so the commentaries; through these narrative words, we shall live in this world and the next. Therefore, stand before <term>Sekhel Yashar: "straightforward intellect" or sound common sense, often contrasted with overly complex or forced interpretations.</term> and the simplicity of the author. He has an intellect associated with a place or places where he knew their order, such as the petty kings in his generation who were in a part of the land. Their word shall not depart as long as the law established in the religion believes alongside them. 

Through these, they will help to bring other books, if people who understand the words of **our Sages of blessed memory** <note>original: *Razal*, another acronym for the Sages.</note> are able, by right, to ascend in honor to that height which is before God. Behold, they stand; if they stand, He will recount their likeness, and a **Book of Remembrance** <note>original: *Sefer Zikaron*. A biblical concept of a record kept by God of those who fear Him, here applied to the preservation of history and scholarship.</note> shall be written before Him by the hand of law, according to the spirit of the understanding of science <note>original: *binat ha-mada*, referring to the integration of religious wisdom and worldly knowledge.</note> and its greatness at the head.

### From the Ninth Degree, etc.

The ninth degree consists of books that contain and concern [these matters]; they too shall be included here strongly among them. They serve to understand the <term>Peshat: the literal or "plain" meaning of a text, as opposed to mystical or allegorical layers.</term> of the <term>Ma'amad ha-galgal: the "position of the celestial sphere." This refers to astronomy and the medieval Jewish study of how the movement of the stars and planets reflects divine order.</term> and the word of God in the holy chapters, which deal with the deepest subjects. These books discuss His ways through the word of the Lord that are known in public, and they add stories of the province where we dwell among those things found there. They shall write "peace forever" for these books.

## The Fourth Act

And you shall dwell there for two weeks among righteous and pious men in prayer. Your reading shall be in the midst of the congregation, and you shall be a leader to guide and to remove the wicked. And behold, I was awakened by these things, and I rejoiced at the coming of the light on the morning of the third day. They said to me: "If you are a God-fearing person and have also descended to the deep place and sought wonders, behold, you shall return and see what you desire in this world—a state of humility to live here, and also to show you the face of the Blessed One." And He shall be with me always, and a hint shall be in my mouth that issued from the Glory <note>original: *Kavod*, referring to the Divine Majesty or the radiant presence of God.</note> to the man as written above regarding the men who are the gatekeepers in the House of the Holy One, Blessed be He. And I praised the Lord who graced me with this degree, that I might see what is in the heart of the person who hears the whole. And those who heard said to him: "Make your hearts straight; turn to the way of the Lord."

Trust in life, and one's fortune, vitality, and faith shall be established forever; and may our portion in life be with the righteous and the pious.

And the second way is the path of the Sovereign <note>original: *al-Sultan*</note> who is hidden in His essence; and what has been attributed to Him in description is only because His essence itself cannot be attained <note>A common theme in medieval philosophy: God's true nature is beyond human comprehension, so we can only describe His actions or attributes.</note>. And thus, in the introduction, is the saying of the philosophers who described the Creator <note>original: *al-Bari*</note>—may He be exalted and glorified—for there the description <unclear>for this to my...</unclear> stands. This is the clarification of His words regarding Peace <note>original: *al-Salam*. In both Jewish and Islamic traditions, "Peace" is considered one of the Names of God.</note>, for He says in the Book: "The Living who does not die." When He named Him "The Living," this refers to the Life that never departs from the Living Essence; it is an attribute within His essence and is not separated from the Essence. This is the specific decree in what was called the **Decree of Lights** <note>original: *Hukm al-Anwar*. This likely refers to Illuminationist philosophy (*Ishraq*), which views the universe as a series of emanations of light from the "Light of Lights."</note>. 

And if this attribute of Peace is validated, then the Creator is the one who has described Him with beneficence in His book, saying: "And He is the one who is God in the heaven" <note>A reference to the theological concept that God’s authority is universal, often citing scriptures like Quran 43:84 or similar biblical sentiments.</note>. And He also said: "The Living in His essence," for there is no escape from Him being the Living; the Living of the Essence is the truly Living. And in the end He said: "The Living who does not die," and He is only in the earth according to the judgment of what is said: "Since *Yah* <note>A shortened, mystical Name of God found in the Hebrew Bible.</note> is what He rules." And when He is the King in what is said on the earth—both in His Book and elsewhere—then if He is named the King, there must be a kingdom, and that is the earth and what is in the abode of this world.

So these are the kingdoms that are in this world and the predecessors of the past; they exist within the essence of **The Holy One, Blessed be He** <note>original: *Ha-Kadosh Barukh Hu*, the most common Hebrew rabbinic title for God.</note>, who was the High Sovereign in this world according to the specific decree. And whoever denies this increases nothing but his own certain end, and no other. It has become clear to you in this Peace <note>or "this salutation"</note> that He was in Peace in Paradise <note>original: *al-Gannah*</note>. And thus His wisdom occurred in the world, and Adam was like one clothed in light <note>A mystical tradition suggests that before the Fall, Adam and Eve were clothed in "garments of light" (*or* in Hebrew) rather than "garments of skin" (*or*—a homophone in Hebrew).</note>. 

So do not wonder at what the Virtuous One <note>original: *al-Fadil*. Likely referring to a specific philosopher, possibly Al-Farabi or Maimonides.</note> said regarding the "Wisdom which is by the King," because the Sovereign is the Majestic King. He is the one described in the Scripture as the Creator who is said to be in Paradise, and the Throne <note>original: *al-Kursi*, representing Divine authority and the boundary of the physical world.</note> which is in this world. Regarding this, he said: "The Creator commands," for He rules the lower world along with the people of Gehenna <note>original: *al-Gahannam*; Hell or the purgatorial realm.</note>. Thus the matter is within His essence. And in the life that was in the world, the dawn of that is completed in this decree regarding this matter, by the saying: "The King, the Sovereign who does not die," in the judgment of His essence. What is attributed as "Life" to the decree is an attribute, for Life is that which is the attribute in the Creator, Exalted and Glorified.

Clothing is not merely a covering or a shield against the harms of heat and cold; because garments <margin>9</margin> are compressed and varied in their types of colors, they serve as a medium. Just as everything that is hollowed out from the body requires an increase in the quantity of the garment and a tightening, so too, for one whose body is heavy, his clothing should be thin and tight. Indeed, the Pious One <note>original: *He-Hasid*. This likely refers to a specific ethical authority, such as Rabbi Judah the Pious, or a general archetype of the righteous man.</note> said that wealth and fulfillment are properly of the body, even if it is found only on one side. One should desire that their clothing be beautiful and praiseworthy, for they are the **servants of God** <note>original: *Avdei Hashem*</note>. For he gave a parable: His servants are close to Him in his rank, and they uphold the face of the state <note>original: *ha-medinah*. In medieval philosophy, this often refers to the "Polis" or the social and cosmic order.</note> through His excellence. Through them, His excellence and the excellence of His wisdom and His Torah are recognized. 

And in the chapter *Arvei Pesachim* <note>The tenth chapter of the Talmudic tractate Pesachim, which deals with the Passover Seder and ethical conduct.</note>, they said to his son Joseph: "Learn from the wisdom of this man if he wishes his words to be heard in the world." For one who despises the body ruins his own righteousness; in the matter of the service of God and the knowledge of God, this body of a human being is the vessel in this world. 

Learn about the "garments" of the soul from the excellence of **Charity** <note>original: *Tzedakah*</note> and **Loving-kindness** <note>original: *Hesed*</note>. For all of these are the excellence of the body and its crown, serving its work in the House of Eternity <note>original: *Beit Olam*, a common term for the afterlife or the Temple.</note> and in His Temple of Glory, with honor and preciousness for the highest rank. All these qualities of majesty, and the deeds of the Blessed One, are the splendors. He placed all these attributes within His rank. Furthermore, it is fitting that the honor of the rank—even for the living garments—should teach the strength of their standing and their endurance in the proper way. This is to show the beauty of their service and their readiness, and their character and rank in this world according to modesty. 

They shall act well and receive in their designated times, and from all matters of the body, goodness, beauty, and the measure of balance shall come—except on the **Sabbath** day, when according to one’s status, the rank is greater than what is normally required across all measures. And not only then, but also on the Days of Remembrance <note>Rosh Hashanah</note>, the months, and the Festivals of the Lord; on the first day, and in the secret of the **Days of Mercy** <note>original: *Yemei Rahamim*. This refers to the forty-day period of repentance from the start of the month of Elul until Yom Kippur.</note> and the law of the forty days. From all that we have said, and from everything, there shall come into his rank the excellence of the body and its praise within all ten degrees, which establish the qualities of joy and tranquility. And these are the foundations, like a spring within it, of the Second Degree. And this is:

### Regarding the Second Degree, briefly

This degree contains much wisdom, and because of it, among those faithful to His rank—whether through tranquility or in the leadership of the state—it serves as a vision and a rank for the state and for all.

In the chapter **"People of the City"** <note>original: *Benei Ir*. He wrote in the **Siddur** <note>Daily prayer book.</note> of the custom to write it thusly, and the **Tur** <note>Rabbi Jacob ben Asher (c. 1269–1343), author of the *Arba'ah Turim*, a seminal code of Jewish law.</note> would follow his paths to speak of the removal of the [letter] *Yod* from "before me" in its cantillation at the end of the **Verses of Praise** <note>original: *P'sukei d'Zimra*. A section of the morning prayer service consisting of Psalms.</note> from the general rule of what he wrote. 

And the **Tur** said that they neither read it nor wrote it in the compilation of custom and grammar until he would "rise in his dwelling" for the sake of his longing, and it would be in his hand to me in his reading in the "House Together." And it shall be written from it in the method of "His Praise," for in all the **Kaddish** prayers <note>A hymn of praises to God found in the Jewish prayer service.</note> in the standing before the **Lord of the Place** <note>original: *Hashem HaMakom*. "The Place" is a rabbinic name for God, implying He is the location of the world.</note>. And the **Life of the Worlds** <note>original: *Chai Ha-Olamim*. A divine epithet.</note> is included in His unique Name, His prophets, and His righteous ones; the nations turn to Him. From this, in the arrangement of the **"Kingship" prayers** <note>original: *Malchuyot*. A central section of the Rosh Hashanah service.</note>, they shall write likewise in the name of the "Chronicles." They took with him the "Face of a Thousand" in his **Machzor** <note>A prayer book for the High Holy Days and festivals.</note> and in the arrangement of some of the customs of their writings. He explained the **Unification** <note>original: *Yichud*. The declaration of God's oneness.</note> and the **Peace** <note>original: *Shalom*.</note>, and His remembrance, and His Name is Truth. And thus shall the **Halakha** <term>Halakha: The collective body of Jewish religious laws derived from the Written and Oral Torah.</term> emerge.

And the **Secret of the Zohar** <note>A reference to the *Zohar*, the foundational work of Jewish mysticism (Kabbalah).</note> [is revealed] when my watch arrives. "Flowing Myrrh" <note>original: *Mor Deror*. A reference to a biblical spice, often used as a metaphor for a great sage or divine emanation.</note> is the King, and I remembered: Blessed be His praised Name in the mouth of all Your servants, in the Holy Name of His unique path and His praised Name. He shall cut it in the writing of the sign; they shall treat it lightly and awaken him. For in all their places they shall call out before Him: "Luminous one with the chosen ones, uplifting themselves in every salvation, not by our own strength." 

And he wrote in the **Machzor** that nothing should be lacking; but it is not correct for the end or between the blessings. For the miracles of trials are in signs, and the inheritances of their kingdoms they designated, for no man has [such] lips. And until now, it is enough for the peace of the custom in the restoration of this custom in all the war, in the name of "my salvation," to subtract in their places the **Confession** <note>original: *Viduy*. The prayer of repentance.</note>. And may the Lord be for my support, surrounding Him, and my eyes are suspended like one who has a vision before him, truly through kindness. His kingdom and His way shall be spoken of, and it shall be correct before the **Blessed King**, if they write in the **Splendor of His Glory**.

<meta>This page continues the philosophical inquiry into the "World of Intellect" mentioned on the previous page, shifting from the nature of angels to the human pursuit of wisdom and the distinction between physical beauty and spiritual strength.</meta>

### On Wisdom and the True Nature of Strength

And everything beneficial, as well as fortune and the beauty of women, is—according to the **wise** <note>original: *al-uqala*. Referring to philosophers or those who use their intellect.</note>—considered ill-omened and censured. All such censure, and its interpretation and its reinforcement, is like the "slaughtered kings," except for the strong; and the strong are likewise inclined toward these things and desiring them. 

But as for the speech of the **common people** <note>original: *al-awamm*. The non-philosophical masses.</note> which is heard in their shops—saying, "By God, I am tired!" and similar phrases—this stems from the foundations of the material world; but for others, it does not. And when we reach the foundations of matters, which are the root and the truth, I have found in all these things nothing except **strength** <note>original: *al-jaborah*</note>, power, and **wisdom** <note>original: *al-hikma*</note>.

(I mean by **wisdom** <term>Wisdom: In this context, *hikma* refers to the highest form of knowledge that connects the human mind to the Divine.</term> here the **Intellect** <note>original: *al-aql*</note> in the sense of the **Soul** <note>original: *al-nafs*</note>).

As for the rest of these soulful powers, it is possible that possessing them is merely a desire or a deception of wisdom. The "beauty" <note>original: *huri*. A term often used for celestial maidens, here perhaps used metaphorically for spiritual attractions.</note> which is the portion of women, according to the Creator, exists in the sense of the "blood" within the intellects, which is governed by wisdom. Regarding wisdom in these matters—which is found in the Intellect—it is the essence. These are the matters found in the **Glory** <note>original: *al-kavod*</note> of God within the world, and the protection that acts as a rein. 

As for the strength of powers, it is the truth according to the world—the strong and the mighty—except by the Creator of the world, God. For in God is the exalted wisdom and **greatness** <note>original: *al-gedulah*</note>. Indeed, in the truth regarding **Joseph** <note>original: *Yosef*</note>, his nature was a "second wisdom." And if you are worthy, you should be vigilant toward Him. **Peace** <note>original: *al-salam*</note> is found in the world as its Lord intended; it is said that He accepts the wholeness of God and comes to them, and He is called the "reason for the descent."

In God, nothing is named except the **lofty wisdom**. In God and in the world: **Glory be to God the Exalted** <note>original: *subhan Allah ta'ala*. A standard Arabic formula of praise.</note>. And in God, there is but One. For it is said regarding the remembrance of the world, that the world by itself does not benefit. In God—and if you are faithful to the **truth** <note>original: *al-emet*</note>—this is because God in the world is the peace upon the root. It is the place where heavy sins are removed; in it is wisdom, and in it is not merely might or strength, but wisdom. 

God carries all matters... the path of wisdom. In the soul itself, one does not reach the roots unless there is obedience. The truth is found in the **prayer of the righteous** <note>original: *salat al-tzaddiq*</note> in God. Upon the rest of them... and he attains.

->Dismissed<-

It contrasts the mundane concerns of the common people with the pursuit of God's "lofty wisdom" and the "prayer of the righteous."</summary>

### On the Spirit of Phinehas and the Ranks of Prophecy

And if these were reports concerning **Phinehas** <note>original: *Pinas*. This was mentioned previously regarding the art, and it was distinguished by those who differ [on the matter], even if it was in agreement with all of them, though their names for it differ. Before the matter of prophecy, [this soul] was attributed to **authentic prophecy** <note>original: *al-naba'iyah al-sahihah*</note> based on what was mentioned [in Scripture] regarding the power and the prophecies: that they were reports and writings, all of them through prophecy, "**mouth to mouth**" <note>original: *peh el peh*. He said that some of it cannot be found <unclear>..</unclear>

The one who errs in religion corrupts it; therefore, **prayer** <note>original: *al-salat*</note> is necessary within it. In that regard, his excellence is complete, for he perceived the matter of the **sign** <note>original: *al-ayah*. A miracle or a divine verse.</note> which was mentioned. There may be a **false dream** <note>original: *hal makdhuba*. Literally "a lied-about state" or "false vision."</note> within it. We are discussing the **prophet** <note>original: *al-nabi*</note>. And that is because he asked about that which has passed, and sent to him to ask: what is the end of the world? For it is not possible for a human being to know it. In medieval philosophy, this refers to the celestial realm of pure spiritual beings or "intelligences" that exist between God and the physical world.</note> and drew near to the King.

> "And there is that which my soul has found rest in." <note>original Hebrew: *ve-tam hu asher sakhnat ali nafshi*.</note>

-> **The Refinement of the World** <- <note>original: *Mikhlas al-Alam*. This title suggests a process of purifying or distilling the essential truths of the world, or perhaps the "Salvation of the World."</note>

When I compiled this book, I heard the words of the <term>sages</term><note>original: *al-hukama*. This refers to the philosophers and wise men of antiquity whose logic was seen as a path to divine truth.</note> regarding this matter. Nothing is mentioned in it except the words of the sages, because the <term>intellect</term><note>original: *al-aql*. This is the faculty of reason that allows humans to understand divine order.</note> does not differ on a matter if it is of that kind—the things that the world uses at all times, regarding correctness and error, and the reading of books and the copying of them, regarding that which is vain. 

And all that I have mentioned in this book is correct according to those of sound intellect, and I mentioned in it some of those matters that do not change. Nothing was mentioned in it except what is necessary, and it is close to [the truth], rather than spending one's life on something that does not benefit, or where one forgets the very <term>knowledge</term><note>original: *al-ma'rifa*. Often refers to experiential or mystical knowledge rather than just information.</note> he learned. 

He who follows this book will find in it something of the <term>science</term><note>original: *al-ilm*. In this context, it refers to organized spiritual or philosophical knowledge.</note> which it is not possible to explain with the tongue in all these books, for they are beyond material matters. This is the <term>wisdom of the ancients</term><note>original: *hikmat al-aqdamin*. Refers to the foundational Greek or early Near Eastern philosophers.</note> about whom he said in his book what I have already written in this book regarding wisdom and knowledge. In it, there are things [to protect] from those matters that corrupt the soul through error and stupidity.

> "And there, I was healed by them; and this is that which he knows alone, and there is nothing else for him."

And I have collected in this book what the sages have mentioned in the world, excluding things other than the words of the sages, even if it came from a single heart in the world in every art of the arts. I have mentioned in it something of the world which is the <term>truth</term><note>original: *al-haqiqa*. This can also be translated as "Reality," referring to the ultimate spiritual reality behind appearances.</note>, and nothing was mentioned in it except the necessary. I only compiled this book because I saw that the nation <note>Refers to the religious community, likely the Jewish people (al-ummah).</note> had been blinded to this thing, and they were filled with matters that do not benefit except by way of the intellect and the world, and taking and giving [commerce]... 

So I mentioned that to them in this book, and I hope to God that He will bring benefit through it. And I have seen the one I asked about that, and his question: it is the thing that is eternal. For this is the thing that is impossible to explain with the tongue, and may God make us know the truth and let us live within it.

-> Amen <-

...so that we may see, with a brilliant and pure <term>mind</term><note>original: *hawnā*. In the Syriac tradition, this refers to the highest, most intuitive faculty of the human spirit, often distinguished from the analytical intellect.</note>, the splendor of your Godhead, and be worthy to worship you. Furthermore, in the prayer of the soul, those who stand in the place of honor come with one hand—those who are the brilliance of holy and righteous men, the servants of God. In it, the suffering of the soul is combined with a clarity of dreams and visions that is desired and precise. In the seven books, there are those who say: "Even if in one hand, my beloved, I am from him; even if he is brilliant, in one they discern."

And there are those who say that in the <term>radiance of righteousness</term><note>original: *zaliqā d-zadiquṭā*. And they say to me in all these things that in the radiance of the seer and in the whole soul, one is not separated from prayer. Sometimes they look with the body in the manner of a shadow, as one who sees through the hand of the body. A seer is there like a **mirror of prayer**, in which the **image of God** is established. And in this, which is consumed at all times, even in this, he is separated from it; all the gaze that is in prayer is by means of a brilliant prayer.

And there are those who say—as I say—that in some brilliance we see even by means of a vision. The whole word concerns which thing should stand in prayer: the gaze of our mind. Even if in this world it is separated, and in the middle it is seen in the radiance that is in the vision and the brilliance of that which is in prayer. And in this, as if in a vision, the face of the seer is on every side, for such a gaze is a vision. There are those who say of me: "Even if he comes to the gaze."

And in the time when he is with him, in the place we see which stands in the state of being consumed, there is a vision from that which is separated, like one who brings it. Even if in the place of the seer, the gaze of the seer is not separated from the vision in that time, for there are even those who say: "He shall stand upon the power of the vision."

In the heaven of the vision, or that which the vision takes—the vision of the vision from something—the soul sees. Just as the clarity of those who have the vision is a radiance that prepares the soul from the vision...

<meta>This page transitions from the mystical, Syriac-influenced reflections of the previous section into a structured legal and theological summary of the Jewish commandments. The script shifts to a Sephardic Rabbinic hand, and the language is Hebrew. The author organizes the 613 commandments into "General Principles" (Kelalim), relying heavily on the authority of Maimonides' 12th-century work, the *Sefer HaMitzvot* (Book of Commandments).</meta>

## The Fifth General Principle
This section contains eighteen matters that <term>God, may He be blessed</term><note>original: *ha-El Yitbarakh*</note>, commanded to <term>Moses our Teacher</term><note>original: *Moshe Rabbenu*, peace be upon him</note>. Among them is one **positive commandment** and seventeen **negative commandments**. Each and every one of these is interpreted and correctly explained in the **Book of Commandments** by <term>Maimonides</term><note>original: *Ha-Rambam* (Rabbi Moses ben Maimon, 1138–1204), the preeminent medieval Jewish philosopher and jurist.</note>, of blessed memory. Now, the first positive commandment is: 
1. To believe in the existence of the Creator, may He be blessed, and the preciousness of His stature and His loftiness, and that He is the Master of all and the Creator of all, and there is none besides Him.

## The Sixth General Principle
This section contains twelve matters, all of which are **positive commandments**. They are fully interpreted and explained in the *Book of Commandments* by Maimonides, of blessed memory. The first among them is **Commandment Sixty-Two**, which is the positive commandment that the **priesthood** is given to each and every priest of Israel. From this, it is clarified that Aaron and his descendants were made priests forever, as it is written: *And Aaron and his descendants shall stand after him...* <note>A reference to the eternal covenant of the priesthood described in the Book of Numbers and Exodus.</note> This commandment is fixed, enduring, and always recognized by all the <term>Sages of Israel</term><note>original: *Chachmei Yisrael*</note>, particularly those explained in the books of **Tradition** <note>original: *Kabbalah*. In this context, it likely refers to the received tradition or specifically to mystical interpretations of the law.</note>.

## The Seventh General Principle
This section contains seventy-five matters that God, may He be blessed, commanded to Moses our Teacher, peace be upon him. They consist of sixty-nine **positive commandments** and six **negative commandments**. All of these are interpreted and explained in the *Book of Commandments* by Maimonides. They are also found in the **Midrash Tadshe** <note>An ancient, symbolic rabbinic work often attributed to the 2nd-century sage Rabbi Phinehas ben Jair.</note> and in the **Book of the Upright** <note>original: *Sefer HaYashar*. This could refer to one of several medieval works of ethics or midrashic history.</note>. **Commandment Eighty-Eight** is for the priests to eat the fruits of the holy offerings, as is stated in the Torah in many places.

## The Eighth General Principle
This section contains eighty-five matters, all of which are **positive commandments**, and their explanation is in the *Book of Commandments* by Maimonides. Regarding these, the Scripture says: *And it shall be that the place which the Lord your God chooses to make His name dwell there, there you shall bring all that I command you...* <note>Deuteronomy 12:11.</note> This commandment encompasses anyone who offers a sacrifice in the land, for he is obligated to bring it to the **Holy Temple** in Jerusalem, in the Holy Land, as it is written: *Whoever offers [a sacrifice] outside the camp is liable for excision.* <note>original: *Karet*. A spiritual penalty of being "cut off" from the people.</note> These matters are explained in the chapter <term>"He Who Brings a Bill of Divorce from Overseas"</term><note>original: *Ha-mevi Get mi-medinat ha-yam*. The first chapter of Tractate Gittin in the Talmud.</note> and in the chapter <term>"The Gold"</term><note>original: *Perek Ha-Zahav*. The fourth chapter of Tractate Bava Metzia in the Talmud.</note>. However, it seems to me that **Commandment Eighty-Seven** is to eat the holy things, as written in many places.

## The Ninth General Principle
This section contains twelve matters that a person is obligated to perform. Some of them are **positive commandments** and the rest are <term>statutes and judgments</term><note>original: *Chukim u-Mishpatim*. In Jewish law, *Mishpatim* are laws with obvious rationales (like not stealing), while *Chukim* are decrees whose reasons are not fully revealed.</note>. Their explanation is in the *Book of Commandments* by Maimonides, and from them:

<summary>This page organizes the 613 Jewish commandments into "General Principles," citing Maimonides' Book of Commandments and various Talmudic chapters to explain priestly duties, Temple sacrifices, and the core belief in God.</summary>

Stretch out your hands and pray. Whoever takes hold of it <note>Referring to the practice of prayer.</note> with the **right hand** in a new spirit, let him prolong the memory of death—as death itself—just as they liken it to the **altar**. In the holy morning, there are five of them; those are all the times. Three or four, who can complete them?

Regarding the fast of the <term>Lord’s Day</term><note>original: *Hadbshabo* (Sunday)</note>, if you wish to pray, and if you wish for the fast to be by means of fasting, you should pray; not death, but to the place where the sun commanded in the beginning.

Become a hearer; on the fourth day <note>Wednesday, traditionally a fast day in many Christian traditions.</note> he does not hear the prayer of the fast, for like the fast, all prayer is of God and of greatness.

If the fasts come, all should pray. If it seems right to us, let us be those who pray the prayers of God at all times.

Mixed with prayers are beautiful prayers, and with beautiful prayers, the second part of the fast. Whoever is in the fast, let him pray. If you desire, pray and make the morning fast glorious. For whoever is devoted to prayer, it becomes entirely through the fast, so that the prayers may go forth.

In the morning fast, do not speak [common words], just as they pray the prayer of acceptance, as it is full of all the fasts that are revealed and beautiful.

***

Again, every day of prayer, listen and do not speak, so that the fast may be entirely in prayers and in rest.

And all those who pray are mixed together; for prayer, they are all in the prayers of the fast. He who is by way of the fast hears. For whoever becomes the totality of the prayer of the fast, do not let him increase [in speech], but be prepared for a new prayer, all of which reaches the [divine] prayers.

Command beautiful prayers, for all that are in the fast—on the Sabbath day or the fast of the <term>Fifth Day</term><note>original: *Hmisho* (Thursday)</note>—the prayer of the fast is beautiful.

The living word strengthens the prayers by means of fasting. Just as one who is full is pure from all fasts, pray in the fast; let it not be weak, but living as they are.

->**Truly wise is he who hears, O children of life—you who are of the company.**<-

Listen, all you children of the path of truth, because it illuminates us from the hearts of all who have shared in it and followed that path. It is from the state of being comprehended. But from that which is comprehended—from that which is known—it is the **Word** <term>Logos</term> <note>original: *Meltha*</note> which in its essence is **incomprehensible**.

It is, even if it is comprehended, it is for the word of knowledge which in its essence is the Word; it is incomprehensible. They shall be, for it is incomprehensible; it is the Word, so that even if it is known, from that which is known, it is the Word, it is incomprehensible.

From what? Even if it is known, is it that it is comprehended, or that it is not? From that which is known, it is the Word; it is incomprehensible. 

The Word, which is from all time, is in time. It is that which is from everything; it is the Word; it is incomprehensible from that which is known. 

The Word, even if it is known... <note>The scribe uses a series of dots here, perhaps indicating a pause in the source or a moment of reflection.</note> but that it is known, it is known from everything it is.

In what it is, that without what it is, from something it is, what it is, from what it is. 

It is from that which is known; it is, indeed, from that which is known. What is it that is known from that which is known? Of Him, it is what from that which is known; it is what; it is what to them? It is not what is in time. It is what is in time; the Word, the Son, it is. They shall be what it is...

--- Page 140 ---
<meta>This page continues the theological discourse from page 25, moving from the "leaven of Adam" and the historical lineage of faith into a philosophical exploration of "Righteousness," "Freedom," and the "Divine Economy" (management). The text uses repetitive, rhythmic language characteristic of Syriac homilies or treatises.</meta>

# Page 84

Let us show, as is right for them, that in this fellowship—which is also the **righteousness** of the second—they might be perfect. And they are the recipients through whom the **life-giving light** is conveyed, so that in the fullness of truth, the **perfect righteousness** <note>original: "zadqa mshamlmana" - a term implying both moral justice and the fulfillment of a divine order.</note> may be seen. From these ministers, the light of truth shall exist in everything that is justified by the eternal righteousness.

For it was not for the sake of joy arising from a singular unity that this was served, but for the sake of the **Divine Economy** <term>Medabranutha</term> <note>The term 'Medabranutha' refers to God's 'management' or 'governing' of the universe and the plan for human salvation.</note> in righteousness. You, oh faithful one, are to the living faithful one as one of them; and wherever there is a leader, there is management. Regarding the unity which is given to them, honored brothers, the manager shall be answered in one of them, so that he may see righteousness—indeed, righteousness itself.

The hope of **freedom** <note>original: "khirutha" - a central concept in Syriac theology regarding the free will and spiritual liberty of the soul.</note> is seen in you; salvation is made visible by the pious manager of the perfect righteousness. And righteousness must first be in his heart; it is the righteousness of one who is free, so that they may see the freedom of righteousness.

This is a perfect demonstration, because the perfect manager appears as a confession of perfect purity. This is the measure of **piety** <note>original: "phyusa" - derived from the Greek word for persuasion or conviction; in this context, it refers to a state of settled faith or piety.</note>. Towards the luminous ones, **Adam** saw that there is also a likeness of these things which belong to God—it is joy. It is the joy of life along with the good gift of righteousness upon his soul. But he was not a commander, nor one who commanded perfect righteousness so that they should serve God through it—He who was poured out for his knowledge of freedom. 

Even if it was poured out from the Eternal Being, it is not completed in God for them; rather, He commanded freedom as a unity, arising from salvation, to be served. To them, the perfect ones of the descent of the **Saviors**, even if they show the established orders in the God of freedom.

But he to whom freedom is perfected shall show the demonstration in these things; those who are drawn from service were helped by it, as if from the building of righteousness. It was poured out in the **management of righteousness** when he shows the freedom given to them. Righteousness is given so that even from the suffering unity within them, they shall praise God, who honors a full freedom from His own grace and from the intellectual power. 

This is the demonstration of God, which is joy to them. <unclear>How long</unclear> they shall serve them! They are perfect in the unity of freedom. But the salvation of the living, which was employed for their sake by God, comes from visible things in a single perfection of the Savior. From that which exists first, he is a dweller in God, moved by the thought of freedom—freedom as He Himself is. The Knower is worthy, so that in this too he might show the piety that follows, which he shall not complete apart from the Divine Economy.

<margin>Righteousness and Freedom</margin>

<summary>This page explores the relationship between Divine Righteousness (Zadqa) and human spiritual freedom (Khirutha), arguing that true freedom is found through the "Management" or Economy of God. It links these concepts back to the figure of Adam and the "luminous ones" as models of perfect piety.</summary>

<keywords>Righteousness, Freedom, Divine Economy, Adam, Piety, Salvation, Unity, Syriac Theology</keywords>

->As you have already heard from the mouth of the Philosopher <note>original: "Ha-Filosof" — in this context, refers specifically to Aristotle.</note>, and you must know this:<-

That this perfect <term>Form</term> <note>original: "Tzurah" — in Aristotelian philosophy, 'Form' is the essence or defining nature of a thing, as opposed to its physical 'Matter.'</note>, as well as the <term>Perfection</term> <note>original: "Shelemut" — refers to the ultimate state of completeness or the fulfillment of a being's potential.</note> of which he spoke, are certainly those well-known pleasant things, and that it has its own distinct virtues. And behold, among the ranks called human beings, this is not found; and he whose soul is not disciplined and is loathsome—belonging to that nature which belongs to those [who lack discipline]—cannot have a portion in this, except perhaps in some partial sense. Do not trust at all that it exists in the manner that was given to us and thought of [by the common people], for they are foolish, especially regarding any aspect of the <term>Nomos</term> <note>original: "Nimus" — derived from the Greek *nomos*, meaning law, custom, or social order.</note> which is decreed. I have found that the most excellent kingdoms possess the proper perfections and virtues in this regard. And since we have mentioned the Philosopher and mentioned the conduct that governs the aspect of the body which is joined to the soul, we have also perceived that we must awaken [the reader] here and make it known, with simple agreement: there is an excellent form and eternal rest in this Form, though it is not found <unclear>here</unclear>...

From now on, every part [is included] under the name of this Form, which is a partial perfection, and it is found in these things. Perhaps all the virtues in their totality are what indicate that this perfection is partial and found in these. What should further be added to these words is that one must understand the name of this excellent Form. And how would you see it if you thought that if a man among men were to look into this and perform for himself a craft or a business, whether he was successful or more secure, or the like—if such things as these [were his only goal], it would not enter the heart or the mind, except if this Form was weak in him or the like. [This is true] regardless of any knowledge, speech, rumor, or that which is known from the example of the Philosopher, however excellent it may be. Even if the fruit of the statement and the action mentioned were in his heart or in his deeds—engaging in and **healing bodies**, and conducting himself with his intellect and his time, being famous for this among men—or any other crafts or deeds belonging to the wise, or the good things belonging to security, or the perfection belonging to practical matters, or anything besides these that he appointed, or other strange things like the **honor of princes** and **dominion**; in these things, a man has his shadow, his honor, his joy, and his great pleasure from all this...

---

From now on, every craft under the name of "Form"—whether in general or in particular, in all names and among all the multitude—and every business, action, speech, imagination, and will: everything goes toward it and everything dwells within it. It is found in the <term>Wisdom</term> <note>original: "Khokhmah" — here referring to Sapience or the highest intellectual virtue.</note> of which we speak. Thus one shall aim, and thus it shall appear, and thus his handiwork will appear successful to him, and they shall be [successful] and together they shall be **complete** and **perfect**.

<summary>This page explores the Aristotelian concept of "Form" and "Perfection," arguing that true intellectual or essential form is superior to practical crafts, political power, or the "honor of princes." It emphasizes that all human actions and wisdom should ultimately aim toward this higher, "perfect" state of being.</summary>

# 7

O Lord, the God of my life, and my submission from my creation; a great division has been brought upon them. For I know that at this time, from the passions of the flesh, I am mourning here. Since the appearance [of things] is being divided— <note>original: "p-l-g," referring to the internal division or struggle within the soul between worldly desires and spiritual focus.</note> 

one takes hold in the tranquility of thoughts and perceives those things which are right for their minds beforehand. They are all known to us, and from the former things which were said to be "creepers." <note>original: "tawlaya," likely used here metaphorically for base or "earth-crawling" thoughts/passions.</note>

## From the Fifth Discourse of Saint Isaac
<note>Saint Isaac of Nineveh (Isaac the Syrian) was a monk and bishop known for his writings on the inner spiritual life and the "law of the Spirit."</note>

The rivers of the law of the flesh are one [side], while opposite them is the law of the illustrious Spirit, which is full of all the wisdom of the illustrious one, to confirm the division of truth and the distinctions of truth. For these are the mediators. What is it, O brother, of the mystery and in the likeness of the distinctions of the season of the sun? Within its spread is the prayerful form of salvation. <note>Isaac often uses the "sun" as a metaphor for divine illumination or the presence of Christ within the soul.</note>

Illustrious ones! From these words of truth which were previously revealed, known good things are prepared. Against those of the faith of the whole, which the body has taken hold of like a child; all that belongs to knowledge is of use and is temporal in the life of the dwelling-place of the will. In it, the confirmation exists in its first image—the joy of that which is to come. Rejoice in the salvation through our Savior. Amen.

## From the Discourse "Do Not Err"

And of the illustriousness of the hidden things and the thoughts which are from the Father. I am God, who in your peaceful flesh, the Word knows that which even the ministers—or in the likeness of the luminaries—see. The flesh is glorified in it, the high faith of the chosen one over the flesh. In the revelation, indeed, God is divinized! <note>original: "m'alah Alaha," a reference to theosis or divinization, a central concept in Eastern Christian mysticism where the human person is united with God's grace.</note> 

Within the sun, by His grace, all times [are held], because their mystical sufferings are like the sufferings of our Lord Christ; by their life, the souls shall be spread out. For them, the reduction of it is fitting, from what belongs to me, O Father, in the joys of the mind in this thought. We are a unity; to Him in the name which is from before. May He enlighten and give rest to all, let Him give rest! To us for the life, for He is the one who lives. God, from all His cares, is life. His name from it is illustrious in the mysteries. Because it is like the distinctions of the [spiritual] place, the gladness which the Word of the generation has given, which He gave to the first illustrious one—whose time fell into the commandment of the time of all.

-> Who complete the praise to the Father and the Son <-
-> and to the Holy Spirit, Amen. <-
-> ♰ <-

--- Page 149 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Fifth Discourse" of Saint Isaac of Nineveh, transitioning from the introductory metaphors of the "sun" and "rivers of the flesh" to a deeper meditation on spiritual rest (repose), the divine nature of the Word, and the ascetic struggle of the saints.</meta>

# 7

Before you find rest, O little ones, in a place as is necessary: under the **censers** <note>original: "pirmē," referring to liturgical incense burners or the smoke of worship</note> of the nations in silence.

Rightly simple is that which was praised and is praised for the sake of the illustrious glory and is near to God and His rewards. And it shall be known openly from what was said to them, O brothers: the divine, distressed saviors are revealed. And He is praised by men, and demons, and angels, from what they said concerning everything—that He might be the first who exists.

And also this one, whom they took thither, the motherly ones: you, our brother, shall be a servant for the Mighty One alone. Thus began the **repose** <term>repose: "men-khutha," a central concept in Isaac’s theology referring to a state of spiritual stillness and resting in God</term> blowing in him, in that simple thing which is not of the race of His rewards from God. 

Regarding the rewards (if we see them), they are something divine; because where (if we see them, we say so) they are distinct from those who are of the race of God (just as they are). And they appear in their **persons** <term>person: "qnoma," a Syriac theological term for an individual substance or hypostasis</term> like the Lord, the King of them all. He is something of the glory which is not graspable <note>Incomprehensible to human perception or the senses</note> by our passions (if you have seen them), he said to them.

O distressed divine ones! God, who is the entirety of **Being** <term>being: "ithya," referring to the divine essence or existence</term>, He who fills the heavens and the earth, and He who is the Judge:

->As He is from His own soul:<-

But for this reason, by what means does He return even to the Lord? When He came, He who left everything of wealth and righteousness in this **substance** <term>substance: "qayuma," referring to existence, standing, or a state of being</term>, they understood their place and appeared. And it was sweet—for I know this—praising God the Word from His own humanity. And he said to them: "I know that only the believers [know this]." For while there is pestilence, and when this shall come to be: under this which appears in littleness (you are able to find comfort) in the things they rested in.

Until the **struggle** <term>struggle: "aguna," the spiritual contest or "agon" of the ascetic against passions</term> of God, not even because of this shall it be in their power over them, so that they should also be in distress and fear. Praised be our Lord! You who are from Him in the creation of Jesus Christ, we are the fast of the sacrifice and of the resting. It is seen from the victory which belongs to the Living Son, who gave life to all whenever [He came] to the saints.

The praised holiness in heaven, and on earth, and in the sea—as He appears according to **nature** <term>nature: "kyana," the essential or natural character of a being</term> in the world—so that it might be that when you have seen the saints, they are taken with the whole soul to the Savior. They are the holy, the righteous, and the pure. They say to Him, the Word of God, who is under the **censer** <note>original: "pirma"</note>, who spoke to Him in His threefold kingdom forever and ever.

Amen. When You shall be praised among His saints, Amen. Your sign, O illustrious and holy brother...

That the practice of **Antony** <note>Saint Antony the Great (c. 251–356), the "Father of Monks" whose life defined early Christian asceticism</note> (or of those like him) might be manifested; those who were unique in the way they managed their households, so that they might stand as **orderly examples** <note>original: "taqnē," meaning regulations, standards, or well-ordered examples</note> for those on the outside. And so that those within might be living examples in opposition to the trials [or times] of training. From this, then, it is seen that he separated even his hopes. 

And see, it is not by **word** <term>word: "mellta," referring here to speech, theory, or external profession</term> alone that it is right for us to appear to have separated from evil thoughts, but also in **deed** <term>deed: "ʿbādā," referring to practical action, labor, and the actualization of virtue</term>. 

For this word is not praised by word alone, but by deed; and not by word alone. The author comes from a supported soul through the word; not that God, but the angel sees. And he exists in deed like the soul, but from the soul the **fear of God** is born, when **discernment** <term>discernment: "pārōšā," the ability to distinguish between spirits or between good and evil thoughts</term> is in it—of those who possess or fulfill words. 

And to that disobedient word, no. Who is not obedient by the word of its nature? The word? But rather this: those who continually fulfill the word. Oh, who is beautiful? Everyone sees that outwardly, within the body, the seers labored—not merely to lead souls as a man sees them outwardly, but that they might labor so that what is praised outwardly is separated from it. Therefore, it was completed in the body; those things from within were beautifully revealed in deed, which are in the outer life. For it is praised from the outside, but it is in deed alone—yet it is in word alone that it is right for us to appear, so that the means might be praised of those who are in opposition to those who are within. What word?

<meta>This page continues the discourse on spiritual discipline, shifting from the practical example of Saint Antony toward a high-level philosophical investigation into the nature of Being and the Divine Will. It mirrors the style of Syriac translations of Greek philosophy (like Dionysius the Areopagite or Aristotle) often used by East and West Syriac mystics to explain the mechanics of the soul's relationship to God.</meta>

Therefore: from each and every part, he takes and separates from **Being** <term>Being: "Ithya," a technical term for absolute existence or the Divine Substance</term>, namely from the heavens and the earth. 

Also, he is to be disciplined in that which is studied <note>original: "metdarshana," referring to a subject of study, training, or contemplation</note>, so that from those things which are brought forth, those things which are **fitting for God** might be known. And how, in that which is subject to discipline, should the **Will of Being** <term>Will: "Sebyanā," the creative or governing intention of God</term> exist? It does not belong to a single **time** <note>original: "Zabnā," referring to chronological or linear time</note>, even if it is called **intelligible** <term>intelligible: "Metyadʿana," referring to the realm of the mind or spirit, perceptible to the intellect rather than the senses</term>. 

And how is it [Being] such that from it may be found the things known? May they bring the knowledge of how all that has come to be exists. And how is it, if it is **Being**, that it is the return of those things toward which the **Will of Being** has gone? Not even in one [way], even if it [the Will] is towards the **Existent** <note>original: "Ithyatha," plural or feminine forms of Being/Existence</note>, as that which is called "the known" was known by them. If **Being** is capable of being felt <note>or "grasped," original: "metmseyana"</note>, how then does **time** proceed from **Being**? Even when **Being** was being known from all these works, **Being** is to this mother <note>likely referring to a philosophical "source" or "matrix"</note> that which exists so that it might be found [as] a descendant to this one. These things, too, I have said from us: if His **Will** has gone forth, it is the return of the silence <note>original: "shuthmā," referring to a state of stillness or silence</note> concerning every word that exists. And from every Lord through whom God has come, everyone of them exists in the Lord.

If it is completed from those things which are spoken regarding these heavens, and the people of **Being** <note>the "Ithyayē," those who belong to the realm of true existence</note> in their study, they are released in every God-given **time** which knows if **Being** is in anything. Not even in one treasury <note>original: "gazzā," a storehouse or treasury of wisdom/being</note> shall **Being** be known by them, except through His **Will**. How, even if in every single person <note>original: "parsopā," the outward appearance or personhood</note>, the name does not make it known?

-> Completed: if anything was known, whether it exists <-

To him, while **Being** [exists], how is the whole of **Being** completed? Even if the building <note>or "sonship/structure," original: "bnyānā"</note> goes forth, and everything which exists is in **Being**. Even as it is [expressed] in the word of understanding: **time** is spoken of in **time**. Which [one] is it, if he delivers [one] from them? And its return is prepared for him, from how also **Being** shall exist. And she is beautiful from those [things], and **time**, except how **Being** is prepared for this from a single known thing. 

If it exists <gloss>if anything exists</gloss> from **Being**, it was also known. <gloss>If anything</gloss> was known <gloss>if Being</gloss>. Something was known <gloss>if anything of Being</gloss> while it was being known <gloss>except</gloss> the **Will of Being**.

## The Lord of the Adversary <note>original: "Mare d-Ba'eldaba." This term "Ba'eldaba" literally means "Master of the Lawsuit" or "Accuser," often used as a title for Satan or a spiritual enemy</note>

May sin be removed from the hands. First of all, just as people begin these prayers for their wives, so that they might be in the likeness of the prophets—just as they pray their own prayers, so too where their wives pray, as it is said in the prophecies (or in the prayers of the prophets as they prayed)—so that the power of God might be seen in them. For the time has come for the Word of God to be served by both men and women constantly. As the Lord of All said through the prophet Joel <note>Referencing the Book of Joel 2:28</note> in that which he said: "I will pour out my Spirit upon all flesh, and your sons and your daughters shall prophesy, and they shall see visions, and dream dreams." And regarding the tribes: it is so that they might be delivered from every covenant and from the blood of lust <note>original: "dma d-zahuta," referring to impurity or carnal desires</note>.

And I say to us, those who are in the prophecies, that not even one of them was ever given a womb <note>original: "awba," can mean bosom or womb; the text suggests a spiritual birthing or receptive state</note> by any of the gods <note>The plural "gods" here likely refers to pagan idols or false powers</note>. This, in its likeness, is constantly delivered from the hands of sin. And these who pray on behalf of those "asked for by the Evil One" <note>original: "sh'ayl bisha." This mirrors the biblical concept of the Adversary "demanding" to sift someone like wheat</note>, and those who are emptied of sins, not just from time to time—God, who knows all, [knows] how our lives are called by the God who is the Son <note>original: "Bra," specifically the Son in a Trinitarian or Christological sense</note>. 

And the breath <note>original: "Pama," likely the Syriac "Pumā" (mouth/breath)</note> that saves many of them in the times—if they see life, it comes upon those who pray these prophetic prayers, so that they might be delivered from the adversary who is against us. It is for us to go out to heaven and to see, even those who pray on his behalf, if they shall see the Son. 

<margin>The manner that was released</margin>

They are helped, even they themselves, before Him, in that they take care that their souls become pure, all of them being cleansed without blemish. And those who care for these prayers, their souls are prepared in joys, just as there is the blood as in the prophecies—which is how one is helped against the adversary of the years. Also, when they pray these prayers, the adversary exists during the time of the prayers. It is then that by one of these prayers he <note>the practitioner</note> goes out, just as he has seen God; by which vision he shall become free. Just as they pray in that which he saw from within those who pray to the First One, in whom are all the Living Creatures <note>original: "hayuta," likely the Chayot or "Living Creatures" of Ezekiel’s Chariot vision</note>. Our [nature] is helped and protected from this entrance of all.

->From the vision of those [people]:<-

<meta>This page continues the discourse from the previous discussion on prophecy and spiritual life, moving into the practical and metaphorical reception of "the King" (likely Christ or a high ecclesiastical figure) and the communal conduct of the brothers during times of prayer and fasting. It maintains the focus on the "salvation of soul and body" mentioned previously.</meta>

When it is right and fitting—just as whenever a **King** goes out into the field or a **Lord** returns to his own place—so too, from whatever place one departs from our village, it is right for our brothers to go out to meet him. They should receive from his presence two or three of those whom they wish to be with them. (These are my brothers). And therefore, their living bodies are sustained; the life of their soul and body is like a hero <note>original: "ganbara," meaning a giant or a mighty warrior</note> when the sun rises. 

Because of this, our brothers should immediately come by way of prayer. Specifically, when this occurs during the season of the fast <term>zawma (the prescribed periods of liturgical fasting)</term>, as is our will, let us not fear slaughter nor the plunderer <note>original: "tarufa," meaning a predator or an oppressive person</note>. Rather, before all of them, let us see the revelation <term>galyana (a divine uncovering or apocalypse)</term> of these brothers. It is right for them to be cautious in everything that is fulfilled, as I know from our brothers, my brothers. There is a day in their noble dwellings <note>original: "adirayhun," likely referring to the assembly rooms of the community</note>, as the discerning person knows, regarding that which is fulfilled from one thing to another. In all the places of the saints, you have seen where the brothers are. (And today, I know that you have come).

There is a day in every season when the brothers come, and for the peace of the Church <term>shlama d-edta (the liturgical peace or communal harmony)</term>, everyone who has come goes out. The King has something for them. There are brothers who are more distinguished; he knows them. Therefore, he sends for you, just like those of you who have come, so that with them you may find the salvation <term>purqana (deliverance or healing)</term> of soul and body. And if they are released from all that has been given to us by the saints, those too are the ones who have come, my brothers. 

And peace be to you.

---

There are brothers who, when they depart from all the other brothers, act as ministers <term>mshamsane (servants or deacons)</term> who live by this book, my brothers. Even in the time when you have come, my brothers, it is right for you to be our witnesses <note>original: "hzaya," literally "seers" or "observers"</note> of everything that goes out. Just as everything that goes out from the brothers is known, it gladdens you. You too, brothers, who have come, my brothers, in the time that remains—above all that can be seen by any people—our brothers in their own land, just as he goes out to meet them.

### Instructions for the Morning Prayer

In the **Morning Prayer** <note>original: "Salat al-Fajr," the dawn prayer</note> and likewise in the **Mid-morning Prayer** <note>original: "Salat al-Duha"</note> and others, you should choose for them, in this prayer, themes of holiness, sanctification, status, strengthening, and glorification—simply and plainly, as befits us and the majesty of His holiness, may He be exalted. 

Because their time among the people is spent in the <term>Introduction</term> <note>original: "al-Muqaddimah," referring here to the "Pesukei de-Zimra" or Verses of Song that open the service</note>, you must inform them that their prayer is something greater in the sight of God than **idle talk** <note>original: "al-hadith," referring to mundane conversation during the service</note>. Indeed, the sages have established the reality of prayer therein, which consists of the meanings of praise, glorification, and magnification in a verified manner. This includes the whole of the <term>Qedushah</term> <note>the "Sanctification" prayer where the congregation imitates the angels</note>, the "Holy, Holy, Holy" <note>original: "Qadosh, Qadosh, Qadosh"</note>, the <term>Kaddish de-Rabbanan</term> <note>the "Scholars' Kaddish" recited after studying Torah</note>, the prayers "Between the Pillars" <note>likely referring to specific stations in the synagogue or parts of the service</note>, and what resembles that.

You should inform them in the Introduction what prayer truly is by saying to them: "Know, O my brothers, that in your prayers there is that which is greater in the sight of God and weightier than idle talk." They have established the reality of prayer, which is the meaning found in praise, glorification, and magnification in a verified way. 

It is mandatory for them to do this because they require **explanations and commentaries** on these matters. Since these things require explanation, they should be explained in the Introduction—meaning the entirety of the prayer known as the <term>Pesukei de-Zimra</term> <note>original: "Verses of Song," a selection of Psalms intended to prepare the heart for prayer</note>. 

They have placed this in the Introduction so that it may be chosen for them, to alert them to what is said regarding this truth. It is the duty of the <term>Shaliach Tzibbur</term> <note>the "Messenger of the Community" or Prayer Leader</note> to say to them: "Know, O my brothers, that what you do in this Sanctification <note>Qedushah</note> is a magnification and honoring of the Creator, blessed and exalted is He, when you say: **'Holy, Holy, Holy.'**"

After that, you should also inform them in the Introduction about the phrasing of the prayers and what is said "Between the Pillars" in the entirety of this great prayer. These words are within the "Secret of the Sanctification"—meaning this Sanctification is what magnifies, and within the glorification, He is the Sublime, along with the rest of the glorifications contained within itself. 

It is necessary that the true meaning be understood: that the prayer brought by the worshiper is a unified whole. This is necessary because they do not know the meanings of these words. This is what is required of them from this chapter. The Prayer Leader should teach them this with the most extensive explanation and clarification: the manner of approaching, magnifying, and glorifying in this prayer. These are the things they truly need, which the Prayer Leader must make them understand, for he is the one who **carries their sins** <note>an idiomatic way of saying the leader acts as the legal and spiritual representative of the congregation before God</note>. 

The explanation they need regarding the actions and words in this prayer is this: they must be careful to avoid babbling and excuses. Glorification and magnification are the reality when they say: **"May His great name be blessed forever and for all eternity"** <note>original Aramaic: "Amen, yehe shmeih rabba mevarakh..." the central congregational response of the Kaddish</note>. Their intention in the prayer must be toward <term>Perfection</term> <note>original: "al-Tamam"</note>.

<summary>This page provides instructions for a Jewish Prayer Leader (Shaliach Tzibbur) on how to teach the congregation the spiritual significance of the morning liturgy. It emphasizes that the leader should explain the meanings of the Qedushah, Kaddish, and Pesukei de-Zimra to ensure the community prays with proper intention rather than engaging in idle talk.</summary>

# Before the Service for the Dawn of the Sabbath

And at this opening, there are those who say the prayer of the third hour <note>9:00 AM in the traditional marking of time, often associated with the descent of the Holy Spirit.</note> before the vigil from the prayer book of the dawn of the Sabbath. After they have completed the prayers for the dawn of the Sabbath, [asking] "Who is he from heaven?" we sing that he is the Merciful One. We shall strike the <term>canon</term> <note>original: "qānōnā." It is fitting that this should be done during the Prayer of the Lights. Wherever the entire cleansing of that Opening of the Lights is found, something is changed in the wording. Sometimes they are called the "portions of the lights." And to this, my brothers, we are to vow the "peace." We sing those [hymns] which are said for those to whom the peace was given, who are exchanged, <note>Possibly referring to those who have died or those being substituted in a liturgical role.</note> and for those who are kept away from the food of the fast.

Through Him, Simon <note>The Apostle Peter.</note> heard that for the saints—whether from [suffering] or from the strength of sleep and death—and for everyone who has the witness of life after the prayer, and the prayer of the last Sabbath, [there is] glory. And they did not extend or ask for fear after the resurrection, like that which exists at all times; let the living draw near to the Living One with praise. We sing to you, our God, another canon.

## The Rule for Praying During Food and Drink

It is found among those who pray with "hands of peace," <note>A gesture of prayer, possibly related to the ritual "Kiss of Peace" mentioned on the previous page.</note> and this prayer is offered in warmth and with tears of longing, so that they might approach in the likeness of the saints, through whose hands we receive the intellect <note>original: "hawnā." The "noetic" or spiritual mind.</note>. 

As one has said: a person should pray bodily even during food and drink. Whether it is a matter of the soul, or by means of a prayer that does not have a single thought belonging to the [material] creation, behold, it says to the Lord of the earth—who said that those who are living should be contrite—that they shall be found in the Lord through the Holy Spirit and through the life of all these words. 

Together in the name of God, they should pray and remain silent during use <note>Meaning during the practical act of eating.</note>. They are found in this rule—those people who pray at the time of food. If you desire to know anything concerning the words that reside in the heart of man and their **passions** <note>original: *ḥashē*; in the Syriac ascetic tradition, this refers to disordered impulses, emotions, or the "sufferings" of the soul that lead to sin</note>, for those who possess even a small measure of understanding to know them truly—that they are unchanging and why this is so—consider this: if it is known, the **cross** <note>original: *ṣlibā*; here used metaphorically for the ascetic struggle and the "crucifixion" of worldly desires</note> does not appear to them to be with their own [nature] from the beginning of the heart of the **chosen soul** <note>original: *naphshā gbithā*</note> that is exercised in those things which belong to it, so that it might not be made equal to them in any way, nor to anything of yours, as those who know a man from another's word alone and not from life itself.

They are able to be **watchful** <note>original: *zahirē*; referring to spiritual sobriety or *nepsis*, the state of being alert and awake to the movements of the mind</note> by following those who are disciplined in the ways of the saints, which proceed from the heart. These thoughts come from this matter: in all of them, the thoughts counsel them as if they were their own bread and breath. Because I say that a degree of **watchfulness** <note>original: *zahiruthā*</note> is in them so that they might feel them—not as if he shows them as their own, for some aspects of the "night" belong to them—that is, the knowledge that comes by **grace** <note>original: *ṭaybuthā*</note>. It is not in anything evil that one should be watchful, for such a man does not see this as it is written. As I have said, concerning such matters on the boundary of the mouth, it is not in their power to begin [the cure]; it begins for him in the truth of the passions that are within them. 

Regarding these matters: when you are in the heart, away from beautiful things, let them be **warm** in their heart, in the likeness of those from a household who are profligate in a feast. Like those who find in the heart a way for life in everything—as if comforting those people who are dissipated and who unsettle their own hearts. For every human being, it is by their hand that they begin such things, even in that which is base. It shows them so that they might know them, and be separated from those who are like them.

In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, one God. We begin to write the account of the seekers <note>original: *ṣaydāyē*; literally "hunters," often used metaphorically for those seeking spiritual truth or the Apostles</note>

## Homily of the Saint

By Ephrem, concerning the holy Job: how he, like a divine thing, shone with righteousness. For like the **sun** which shines from the high firmament, spreading light across all regions to illuminate all people with the brilliance of its rays; so also is the holy Job. If one contemplates him with a discerning eye, we see that he enlightens and makes others wise through the intentions of holiness. Therefore, we have seen his wonder in every place where he was. This was even when you <note>The text shifts to a direct address to Job</note> were cast upon the ground in the pangs of disease. Even though his body was cast upon the ground, even though the body was covered in sackcloth and not even the wild animals of the desert would approach where he went, even though in the body there was rot and worms, as he said: "I am one who is weak" <note>A paraphrase of Job’s lamentations regarding his physical decay</note>. To those who see, when he is in the brilliance of the flock and the brilliance of the desert and the righteous, and in the love for the earth—this leads them to the divine nations and to the gifts of God which belong to them when they see them. 

Regarding this righteous man, we see even those who are divine, and they give to others the health of body and soul through their hands, for they see God and they see the discourse <term>Mimra</term> <note>A "Mimra" is a specific genre of Syriac literature: a verse homily intended to be chanted or recited</note> by His will. And they call them "divine," a speech prepared and perfected. For this reason, it is fitting for us to look upon Job, who is a wonder of healing for others; they are purified by him and by his mind. For there was no sign of life <note>referring to his death-like state during illness</note>, yet he remained alive in the gift by which he stood in righteousness and in the fear of God—as he said: "I am" to the nations. Their discourse is in the Truth, for wherever they see holiness, the Spirit of God dwells. Job, through all of them, shone in the faith that he had from God. As shown by this Job, who brings forgiveness wherever he speaks a word, giving it to those who tremble in the fear of God; for praise is found within the discourse of his body that lived. Job is his name concerning the discourse.

-> Amen, Amen. <-
